| #242 | **A DURATION-ONLY EFFECT EXPIRES FROM INSIDE THE RESIDUAL WALK AND OUR DERIVED ORDER TABLE CANNOT SEE IT — WILL, 2026-08-13.** *"its the same problem of when tailwind officially ends and how that affects leftovers and residual damage"*, said of a card where Showdown runs Leftovers, Leftovers, the Infestation chip and THEN `-sideend` Tailwind, while we announce the Tailwind expiry FIRST. Same family as the Trick Room-end vs sandstorm-chip ordering he called earlier. **CHASING IT FOUND THE HOLE IN THE INSTRUMENT, WHICH IS WORTH MORE THAN THE CARD.** `engine/residual_order.js` derives the walk by enumerating effects that own an `onResidual` handler — 42 of them. **Tailwind owns none.** It is in the authority's walk purely because it has a `duration`: `findSideEventHandlers(side, "onSideResidual", "duration")` collects it, and then `battle.js:340` fires the expiry at its SORTED POSITION — `if (eventid === "Residual" && handler.end && handler.state?.duration) { handler.state.duration--; if (!handler.state.duration) { handler.end.call(...); continue; } }`. So every duration-only effect in the format — Tailwind, Trick Room, the screens, Safeguard, Mist, Lucky Chant — expires from inside the residual and is INVISIBLE to the table we derive the order from. The 42-row table is not wrong; it is answering a narrower question than its name suggests, which is the `Dex.forFormat`-is-not-a-legality-filter mistake in a new place. **TWO JOBS, AND THE SECOND IS THE REAL ONE**: place the expiries correctly, and make `residual_order.js` enumerate by `duration` as well as by handler so its row count is the population rather than a subset. **A THIRD DEFECT SITS ON THE SAME LINE**: ours reads `|-sideend|p2: |move: Tailwind` with a BLANK player name where the authority has `p2: B`. **THE POPULATION, DERIVED 2026-08-13 AND FILTERED TO THE REGULATION: 49 effects carry a duration, and 44 of them own NO residual handler of any kind** — so `residual_order.js`'s 42 rows and this set of 44 overlap in FIVE (Encore, Grassy Terrain, Perish Song, Syrup Bomb, Uproar). Every one of the 44 is collected into the walk on the KEY `duration` and expires from inside it. **19 of the 44 announce**, because `removeVolatile` / `removeSideCondition` / `removePseudoWeather` fire `singleEvent("End"/"SideEnd"/"FieldEnd")` and those 19 carry a handler that adds a line: Aurora Veil, Disable, Electric Terrain, Gravity, Light Screen, Lucky Chant, Magic Room, Magnet Rise, Mist, Misty Terrain, Psychic Terrain, Reflect, Safeguard, Tailwind, Taunt, Throat Chop, Trick Room, Wonder Room, Yawn. The other 25 expire silently (Protect, the Guards, the two-turn moves, Helping Hand) and are still ORDER-BEARING, because their `duration--` consumes a position in the same walk. So the fix is 19 lines placed correctly plus 25 silent decrements in the right order, and the instrument must enumerate by `duration` OR handler rather than by handler alone **THE INSTRUMENT HALF IS CLOSED, 2026-08-14 (MEASURE), AND IT CORRECTS THE COUNTS ABOVE — INCLUDING MINE.** `engine/residual_order.js` now enumerates by `duration` OR handler and a row is an (effect, ATTACHMENT SITE) pair, because the site decides the callback name and hence which `*Order` is read. **42 rows became 90 walk participants**: 58 expire from inside the walk, 48 of those own no handler at all, **28 announce** and **30 are silent and still order-bearing**. **THE HAND-DERIVED LIST OF 19 ANNOUNCERS ABOVE NAMES TWO MOVES THAT ARE NOT IN THIS REGULATION — Lucky Chant and Mist are both `isNonstandard: 'Past'`** — and omits the four weathers, `partiallytrapped`, Syrup Bomb, Encore, Perish Song and Uproar; Heal Block belongs on it, reachable through Psychic Noise. **AND THE PREMISE THAT THESE EFFECTS HAVE NO ORDER IS WRONG: they declare one.** `tailwind.condition` carries `onSideResidualOrder: 26, onSideResidualSubOrder: 5` and simply declares no `onSideResidual`; `resolvePriority` reads `effect[cb+'Order']` whether or not the callback exists, so the order was in the format all along and the `if (!hook) return` guard threw the effect away before anything read it. Proved by RUNNING one staged walk on the authority, stable over twelve seeds: sandstorm expiry (1), terrain heal and Leftovers (5), poison (9), `-end` Taunt (15), `-sideend` Light Screen (26/2) then Tailwind (26/5), `-fieldend` Trick Room (27/1); the 29 participants that declare NO order sort at the `4294967296` sentinel, last, by holder speed. **GRASSY TERRAIN IS IN THE WALK TWICE AND ELEVEN ORDERS APART** — heal at `onResidualOrder` 5 with the healed body's speed, expiry at `onFieldResidualOrder` 27 with none — which one row could not express and the 42-row table did not. Four published values were also wrong and all four were one mistake, a rule copied instead of called: `psn`/`tox`/`brn` at subOrder 2 where effectType `Status` gives **0**, and `wish` at 2 where a slot condition gives **3**. The file no longer owns a copy of the sort rule — it CALLS `Battle.prototype.resolvePriority` and `comparePriority` on real staged state, which caught my own replacement publishing Fairy Lock at subOrder 5 when `field.addPseudoWeather` writes no `target` and the live answer is 2. Gated by `tests/test-residual-order-population.js` (14 checks, auto-discovered), which asks the authority's own collectors for the walk with `getKey: 'duration'` and was **shown RED first** — the handler-only enumeration makes it name 41 live participants with no row. `tests/test-residual-order-observed.js` was green throughout and could not have caught any of it: it only looks at effects the table already contains. **THE PLACEMENT LANDED 2026-08-14 (ENGINE) AND THE ROW CLOSES.** Ten clocks moved into the walk at the orders the artifact publishes — the three screens (26/1, 26/2, 26/10), Safeguard (26/3), Tailwind (26/5) on both sides, Trick Room (27/1), Gravity (27/2), Wonder Room (27/5), Magic Room (27/6) and the terrain (27/7) — through one `residualExpireAt(order, ...)` called once per order-group AFTER that group's bodies, because a Side or Field holder has no speed and `comparePriority`'s speed term sits between order and subOrder. **NOT ONE ORDER NUMBER IS WRITTEN IN medicham2**; the side conditions are matched on the move id `sf.sc` is already keyed by, so a fourth screen arrives without an edit. **THE SILENT HALF FOUND A SECOND DEFECT**: Roost is order 25 and announces nothing, and the type it gives back is read TWENTY ORDERS ABOVE by the Grassy Terrain heal (order 5, GROUNDED bodies only) — restoring it above the walk refused the heal on every Roost in Grassy Terrain, with no line to say so, and the comment on that code stated the correct reason while the code did the opposite. Staged on the authority first, at a quarter HP: `|-heal|p1a: Talonflame|124/153|[from] Grassy Terrain` on the Roost click and no terrain line at all on Protect. **AND MY OWN FIRST DRAFT SHIPPED THIS PROJECT'S SIGNATURE FAILURE**: it looked the engine's short terrain id (`grassy`) up against the artifact's (`grassyterrain`), matched nothing and stopped the terrain clock outright — a permanent Grassy Terrain with no line and no counter — caught only because a staging with all ten clocks due counted 7 ticks where 8 were owed; `MEDFAILS.residualTerrainExpiryUnplaced` now fires instead. **Census 575 live / 0 missing to 577 live / 0 missing**, both probes (`doublesSideSpeed` at order 26, `typeRemovedForTurn` at order 25) shown RED on a deliberate break that reverts the placement. **MEASURED, two frozen releases differing in ONE file** (`af85d7a4181e` to `bbbf28c728ee`), 1,200 games / 961 played, same seeds and pairs, both arms run back to back under ONE steering state: `top-tie-first` 193 to **188**, `bottom-tie-first` 191 to **176**, `middle` 598 to **584**, the `ordering` class 191 to **176** games (174 to 168 distinct causes), and the three `|-sideend|...|tailwind` first-divergence causes gone. **A 300-GAME RUN MOVED NOTHING AND PRINTED A BYTE-IDENTICAL CLASS TABLE IN BOTH ARMS** — the shape is ~1.4% of games and a game stops at its first divergence, so 258 games cannot reach it; recorded because that was the run asked for and it would have read as "no change". **AND THE SAMPLE IS STEERED BY THE CENSUS, WHICH NEARLY COST THE NUMBER**: the same after release reads `middle 589 / bottom 209` under the pre-regeneration steering and `584 / 176` under the post, on identical bytes, so two arms either side of a census regeneration are not a before/after. The live tree was SHOWN behaviourally identical to `bbbf28c728ee` rather than assumed to be. `test-engine-diff --n 6000` is 0/6000, both corners 0/6000. **STILL OPEN AND NAMED, NOT SILENT**: six per-body volatile clocks are still spent below the walk — `M.residualExpiryDeferred()` derives and prints `taunt@15, disable@17, magnetrise@18, healblock@20, throatchop@22, yawn@23` — and Encore (16), Perish Song (24), Uproar (28) and `lockedmove` share that block as `condition:` rows, so they cannot appear in that list however wrong their position is. | #243 | **THE LIVE FALLEN COUNT UPDATES AT TURN END, SO A LAST RESPECTS FIRED AFTER AN ALLY DIED THAT TURN IS ONE DEATH LIGHT — WILL, 2026-08-13.** He asked directly: *"does last respects update mid turn if an ally dies first and then last respects hits"*. **In the authority, YES, and the two fallen-count mechanics answer it OPPOSITELY** — which is the part worth holding on to. `Last Respects` evaluates its `basePowerCallback` inside `getDamage` (`battle-actions.js:1375`), i.e. at the moment the move hits, and `side.totalFainted++` happens inside `faintMessages()` (`battle.js:2092`), which `runAction` calls after EVERY action. So an ally that dies earlier in the turn is already counted. `Supreme Overlord` reads the same field ONCE in `onStart` and freezes it in `effectState.fallen`, and never re-reads — so a body dying while Kingambit stands there does nothing for it, and only a Kingambit entering afterwards benefits. **WE MODEL THE SPLIT CORRECTLY AND THE TIMING WRONG**: `att._sf.fainted` is live and `_fallenStuck` is the entry snapshot, exactly right, but `sfA.fainted = fallenCount(...)` runs once per turn in the end-of-turn block, whose own comment says so — *"a mid-turn kill is seen one action late — an approximation, stated"*. Declared rather than hidden, which is the right way to carry a gap, and it is still wrong: Last Respects is **+50 base power per death**, so being one death behind is 100 where the authority says 150, a third of the move off. **INVISIBLE TO EVERY EXISTING TEST**, because the census probe stages the death on a PREVIOUS turn (`"one ally really fainted first 1,101"`) and the whole-game differential pins the dice so the ordering that produces a same-turn kill is rare. The fix is to recompute where the authority does — after each action, not at turn end | CLOSED 2026-08-13 — `docs/ENGINE.md`. `fallenSettle(S)` derives both sides' counters at each of the authority's own moments: at `battleInit` after the roster is stamped, at the top of the action loop beside `flushAfterMoveSpends`/`receiverSweep` and ABOVE the `sideWiped` break, after the last action, and once more in the residual so the Supreme Overlord snapshot includes what the residual killed. One function, four call sites. **Authority measured first** at seed [1,2,3,4]: Houndstone's Last Respects after a Garchomp KOs the Milotic ally earlier in the same turn deals 69 against 36 with the ally alive — x1.917, BP 50 -> 100 — while Iron Head from a Kingambit ALREADY OUT deals 70 against 70, x1.000. This engine read x1.000 on both and now reads x1.980 and x1.000. **The split survived and is asserted**: `ability boostsFromFallen` gained a probe that asserts Supreme Overlord did NOT become live, shown RED under a deliberate break (`_fallenStuck` swapped for `_sf.fainted`) because a probe that asserts a negative passes on the unfixed engine too. Census 561 -> 564 live / 0 missing. Whole-game differential BEFORE (`a2857a1bfd70`) and AFTER (`0c5bb83c5744`), releases verified to differ in exactly one file, same pool, 1219 games, both arms: **220/239 diverged in both, every class row identical to the instance — nothing moved**, which is expected because Mode A's pins rarely produce a same-turn kill followed by a Last Respects, and is not a strength claim. The `Math.min(fainted,5)` at the read site was checked rather than assumed: the authority has NO cap on this move, ours first binds at six fallen allies, a bring-4 game tops out at three, so it is unreachable and is left in place with a comment naming it |
| #244 | **THE ROLLOUT SEED DROPS THE DEAD, SO EVERY PLAYOUT BELIEVES NOBODY HAS DIED — WILL, 2026-08-13, AND HIS INSTINCT REDIRECTED THIS ONE.** I first reported that `board.js` prices Last Respects at a flat 50 because `movePower`'s stub has no `side.totalFainted` (measured: the callback returns `NaN`, the `bp > 0` guard rejects it, and it falls through to the printed 50). **He pushed back — *"wait rollout shouldnt be thinking llike that? it should just be playing the games out right?"* — and he was right.** `rollout_leaf.js` does not score a snapshot; it seeds MEDICHAM from the real board and PLAYS THE POSITION OUT, so `board.js`'s `movePower` is not in that path at all and my claim about rollouts was wrong. **THE REAL DEFECT IS IN THE SEED AND IT IS WORSE.** `buildSide` drops fainted bodies — `if (m && m.fainted) { stats.fainted++; continue; }` — correctly, because a corpse cannot act. `battleInit` then stamps `S.sfA.team = teamA.filter(Boolean)`, which is now SURVIVORS ONLY, and `fallenCount` is `sf.team.filter(x => x.fainted).length` → **0**. So a rollout from a position where two of your team are already dead plays out with Last Respects at 50 instead of 150 and a Kingambit with no Supreme Overlord boost, and the deeper into a game the position is, the wronger it gets — precisely the phase both mechanics exist for. **AND THE FIX FOLLOWS FROM WILL'S STATEMENT OF THE DESIGN, NOT FROM THE SYMPTOM.** 2026-08-13: *"miltanks rollout needs to just play the game out on medicham and have it match showdown perfectly thats the whole point. miltanks just chooses the actions."* That rules out the obvious patch. Threading a fallen COUNT through `battleInit` as an extra field would make this card right and would still leave the seed handing MEDICHAM a position that does not exist in the game — a side with four bodies where the real side has six, two of them dead. **The seed must reconstruct the TRUE position, corpses included**, and let the simulator derive everything it derives in a real battle. Anything the playout has to be TOLD is a second source for a fact the engine already owns, which is the rule this project breaks most expensively. A corollary worth stating while it is cheap: **every other approximation on the seeding path is a defect by the same argument**, and they should be swept for rather than waited for — the seed is the only place a correct simulator can still produce a wrong game. **THIS IS THE PATH MILTANK ACTUALLY USES**, which the `board.js` feature defect is not. `board.js`'s `movePower` fallthrough is real and separate and is the SMALLER half: it affects the feature vector MAG scores an action with, and it is a whole CLASS — 29 moves in this format carry a `basePowerCallback`, and the stub satisfies the weight and speed ones while silently returning the printed value for anything reading side or battle state. **VERIFIED BY: `node tests/test-rollout-fallen.js`** — the register row and the gate that decides it, checked against each other by `engine/register_reality.js` rather than by anyone remembering. | CLOSED 2026-08-13 (the seed) — SEARCH, R13; and 2026-08-13 (turn one) — ENGINE, #246. `rollout_leaf.buildSide` appends the roster's corpses, `battleInit` calls `fallenSettle`, and `tests/test-rollout-fallen.js` proves it dice-for-dice: Last Respects at 50/100/150/200 against a position holding 0/1/2/3 in the ground, with the 0 row as the control that says the fix does not invent deaths. **REACH MEASURED, THEN EFFECT MEASURED**: 8.75% of 183,840 decision points (`data/rollout-fallen-prevalence.json`), converting to an argmax flip on **6.1% of 131 paired decision points** under common random numbers (#278, `data/argmax-paired.json`) — and three of that arm's eight sampled flips have Last Respects on the board. **THE ROW'S OTHER HALF IS NOT CLOSED AND IS NOW #283**, deliberately: `board.movePower` still builds no `side.totalFainted`, so the FEATURE vector prices the move at its floor. A row closed in part is a row whose leftovers have no home, which is the shape #269 left behind and #277 had to be opened for. **The remainder is FIT-INVALIDATING and that was measured rather than assumed** — `engine/feature_fixture.js`'s 58 per-feature column hashes MOVE when `movePower`'s callback path is disabled — so it waits for the refit, which is gated behind MEDICHAM by Will's own instruction |

So the check moved to the seam where both the seeded roster and the real position are in scope. `rollout_leaf.checkFallenSeeded` compares what the engine will actually COUNT — `S.sfA.fainted`, the field Last Respects and Supreme Overlord read — against `board.graveyard[side]`, a record `board.faint()` writes and that nothing on the seeding path filters. | #248 | **A BENCHED POKEMON ENTERS EVERY ROLLOUT AT FULL HP, WITH NO STATUS AND THE DATASET'S MOVES — 2026-08-13.** Swept out of the seeding path beside #244 and it is the biggest of the four. `rollout_leaf.sideTeam` synthesises a benched body as `{species, hp: 1, status: '', boosts: {}}` because `board.bench()` returns SPECIES NAMES and the board keeps no state for a Pokemon that is not on the field — `switchIn` builds a brand-new object each time and the old one is discarded. So a Pokemon that pivoted out at 20% and burnt comes back to the playout clean and whole, on every sample, on both sides. **AND IT DOES NOT CARRY ITS DECLARED MOVES**: the bench synthesis passes `item`, `nature` and `pp` from the sheet and NOT `moves`, so `dmgMon` leaves `buildMon`'s dataset-representative four in place — the identical sheet-reaches-one-consumer-and-not-the-next shape CLAUDE.md records three instances of on 2026-07-30. HP and status are public information in a real battle and the fix is a board-side memory, not a rollout heuristic; the moves half is one field in `sideTeam`. **BOTH CHANGE WHAT MILTANK CLICKS, so each owes its own arm rather than a free ride** | CLOSED 2026-08-13 — SEARCH, BOTH HALVES, AND IT IS THE LARGEST OF THE FOUR. MOVES: `sideTeam` passes the sheet's `moves` into the bench synthesis and `dmgMon` already preferred them — one field, and the CONTROL is that a benched species with no sheet still gets the dataset four, so a closed-sheet game is unchanged. STATE: `board.switchIn` records the OUTGOING body's hp and status into `lastSeen` before it overwrites the slot, `board.benchState()` reads it back, and `sideTeam` seeds from it; the board could not answer this before because `switchIn` builds a new object and the old one was discarded. **BOOSTS DELIBERATELY STAY EMPTY** — Showdown clears stat stages on switch-out, so carrying them would be a new bug wearing the shape of a fix, and the gate asserts it. **PREVALENCE (`data/rollout-seed-prevalence.json`, 14,102 open-sheet bo3 games / 190,378 decision points): a live benched body is hurt or statused at 17.053%. THE MOVESET SHARE IS WITHHELD as of 2026-08-23 — see #402** — the generator resolved a species through `norm()`, which strips the hyphen `MC.mons` keys every forme with, so every forme was handed an EMPTY dataset moveset and scored as "the moves differ". Inflation only, magnitude unpublished; the hurt-or-statused figure is computed independently and stands. Two residues declared rather than glossed: Regenerator and Natural Cure heal SILENTLY on the way out, so the memory is a pre-heal HP and a pre-cure status (erring low on HP, which is the safe direction); and the COUNTERS hanging off a status — sleep turns and the toxic ramp, both of which medicham2 models and carries across a switch — are not seeded at all, which is #267 |
| #249 | **THE SEEDED PLAYOUT HAS NO HAZARDS, NO SCREENS AND NO GRAVITY, AND NOTHING SAYS SO — 2026-08-13.** Third sweep finding. `rollout_leaf.applyField` translates exactly four things onto the seeded battle — weather, terrain, Tailwind and Trick Room — and `battleInit` starts `sfA.hz` absent and `sfA.sc` empty. `board.js` HAS the rest: `startSide` derives Stealth Rock, Spikes, Reflect, Light Screen, Aurora Veil and Safeguard from the setter move and its dex duration, and they are readable through `hasSide`. So every rollout switch-in walks onto a clean field, every screen the real board is under is deleted, and a search that already had to be taught not to spam Protect is being shown a cheaper switch than the game offers. Same shape as the terrain defect §3.7 already closed — a field the leaf was GIVEN never reaching the engine — except here the leaf is not even given it. **AND CHASING IT FOUND A DEFECT UNDERNEATH, WHICH IS WORTH MORE THAN THE SEEDING GAP: `board.noteMove` STARTS EVERY SIDE CONDITION ON THE MOVER'S OWN SIDE** (`board.js:2675`, `if (move.sideCondition) board.startSide(side, …)`) **and four of them belong to the FOE.** Derived from the format, not recalled: `stealthrock`, `spikes`, `stickyweb` and `toxicspikes` are all `target: foeSide`, while `reflect` and `tailwind` are `allySide`. So on every OFFLINE board — the fit's board — a hazard is recorded on the side that laid it. The LIVE path takes the side off `|-sidestart|` (`magnemite.js:647`) and is right, which makes this the fitting-environment-and-playing-environment-must-match rule broken in a new place: MAG's `deadSide` and already-up features were fitted with hazards on the wrong side and are played with them on the right one. **VERIFIED BY: `node tests/test-rollout-seed.js`** — the register row and the gate that decides it, checked against each other by `engine/register_reality.js` rather than by anyone remembering. | CLOSED 2026-08-13 — SEARCH. `rollout_leaf.applySideState` seeds hazards, screens, Safeguard and Gravity onto the seeded battle from the board's own per-side record, called from both leaves. NOTHING IS NAMED: hazards come from the `hazard` tag (which also carries `maxLayers`), screens from `halvesDamage`, Safeguard from `sideBuff`, Gravity from `groundsField`, so a condition added by a future regulation is picked up without an edit. **THE SIDE IS READ, NEVER RE-DERIVED, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE TRAP:** #254 resolved it ONCE at the WRITE, in `board.sideFor`, so the seed takes the board's per-side record straight and calls `sideFor` nowhere — a second flip would have re-introduced #254 one layer up while looking exactly like a fix. `tests/test-rollout-seed.js` asserts placement from BOTH seats for all four hazards, plus a behavioural arm in which all four slots pivot at once (so nobody attacks and any HP that moves is entry damage), which is what makes a double flip fail rather than cancel. **PREVALENCE 13.089% of decision points, and it is almost entirely SCREENS** — 24,082 screen-up points against 522 hazard-up and 488 Gravity-up, which is the right shape for a doubles regulation rather than a surprise. Two residues split out as #268 because they move a FEATURE and are therefore not SEARCH's: the board gives a PERMANENT hazard a duration (one turn offline, five live), and it counts no LAYERS, so a seeded Spikes or Toxic Spikes is always one layer |
| #250 | **EVERY SEEDED BODY CAN FAKE OUT, BECAUSE `_mvActs` IS 0 ON A BODY THAT HAS BEEN OUT FOR SIX TURNS — 2026-08-13.** Fourth sweep finding, and the cheapest to state: `firstTurnOnlyRefused` gates on `mon._mvActs`, `buildMon` sets it to 0, and `dmgMon` carries HP, status, stages, item, ability, moves and PP across but nothing about how long the body has been standing there. `board.js` tracks the quantity already — `turnsActive`, incremented in `endTurn`, and its own comment says it exists so the fit can learn Fake Out without the move being named — and the seed does not pass it. Fake Out is 16,871 corpus uses. Also unseeded from the same hole: choice lock, Encore, Disable, Taunt, Substitute, Leech Seed, the perish count and Protect's consecutive-use counter on the FOE (`protectTurns` is passed for my side only, `buildSide(board, foe, dex, stats)` takes no fourth argument). **VERIFIED BY: `node tests/test-rollout-seed.js`** — the register row and the gate that decides it, checked against each other by `engine/register_reality.js` rather than by anyone remembering. | CLOSED 2026-08-13 — SEARCH, AND `turnsActive` WAS THE WRONG QUANTITY, WHICH THIS ROW PROPOSED. Measured wrong in BOTH directions before a line was written: `endTurn` counts every turn a body is on the field, so a LEAD is already at 1 on the turn it may still Fake Out, and a body that entered MID-TURN is at 1 on the very turn it came out to use the move — which is exactly when Fake Out is clicked. So the count is taken where a move happens: `board.noteMove` increments `moveActs`, ABOVE the `worked` gate exactly as the PP spend is and for the same reason — Showdown increments `activeMoveActions` at the top of `runMove`, above every BeforeMove refusal — and `rollout_leaf.seedHistory` copies it onto `_mvActs`. It needs no assumption about when an adapter calls `endTurn`. Gate `tests/test-rollout-seed.js`, RED first; the observable is the FLINCH read out of a played turn's trace, not the field, because reading `_mvActs` back would only prove the seed wrote what the seed wrote. PREVALENCE 17.655% of decision points had a first-turn-only move offerable in the seed that the game refuses (48,221 points with such a carrier active). ONE GAP DECLARED: a turn spent asleep, flinched or fully paralysed emits a cant line and no move line, so `noteMove` never fires and this undercounts — it errs by leaving Fake Out legal a turn longer for a body that has not moved once since it entered, which is the direction the seed already erred in. The rest of this row's list — choice lock, Encore, Disable, Taunt, Substitute, Leech Seed, the perish count and the FOE's Protect streak — is split out as #269, unfixed |
| #251 | **MAG BECOMES A FUTILITY GATE AND STOPS BEING A LEARNED SCORER — WILL, 2026-08-13.** *"im thinking of cutting mag back in size to say, no fake out after turn 1, no poison on steel, no status moves into gholdengo, no prio moves when farig is on the board... just very simple rules and not the whole convoluted, protect and let my partner ko the threat."* **THIS FIXES AN ORDERING FAULT AND IS NOT MERELY A SIMPLIFICATION.** A learned MAG prunes what is bad ON AVERAGE and runs BEFORE DODUO, so it deletes precisely the actions that are bad alone and good in combination — Fake Out into a spread, Follow Me covering a setup turn, a weak click that baits a Protect — which is the entire class DODUO exists to find. The safe line is FUTILE (no other action rescues it) versus BAD (conditional, must survive to DODUO). **THE GATE MUST BE DERIVED, NOT WRITTEN, AND ALL FOUR EXAMPLE RULES ARE WRONG AS TYPED**: Fake Out is `activeMoveActions > 1` — first turn OUT, not turn 1 of the game, so a Fake Out user switching in on turn 6 may still use it; Corrosion (Salazzle, Glimmora) poisons Steel; Good as Gold is `breakable` and refuses only when `target !== source`; Armor Tail is one of Farigiraf's THREE abilities and exempts Perish Song, Flower Shield and Rototiller. Category right every time, hand-written form wrong every time — the ban-list-of-four shape, and **a prune list is worse than a ban list because it fails silently**: a deleted option leaves no trace in any output. So the gate asks the ENGINE "does this action produce any effect on this board" rather than holding rules. **OPEN SHEETS DO NOT REMOVE THE NEED**: Fake Out's condition is battle STATE and appears on no sheet, and the sheet stops being true — six legal moves change an ability mid-battle plus 11 Mold Breaker carriers and a Mummy, and Knock Off has 4,078 corpus uses. It reads the LIVE BOARD, which on turn one is the same object and by turn four is not. **BUILDABLE NOW — the only stage of the pipeline with no fitted parameters, so the quarantine does not block it.** Needs a counter for what it pruned and a check that a pruned action really was a no-op: an over-pruning gate deletes a good play and nothing catches it, because both sides of every comparison share the blind spot | open — search |
| #252 | **THE FUTILITY GATE CARRIES A DECLARED PREDICTION, AND WILL BROKE THE SAFETY ARGUMENT THE SAME NIGHT — 2026-08-13.** *"of course there is forbidden tech of clicking a prio move when farig is on the board because youre making a prediction of it switching out."* The rule in #251 was stated as "no action rescues it", and that quantified over MY PARTNER'S actions only. Quantified over the OPPONENT'S as well, most of the gate's membership stops being unconditional: **a move follows the SLOT, so switching the slot rescues it.** Priority into an Armor Tail Farigiraf is dead only while that Farigiraf is still there when the move resolves; the same holds for Toxic into a Steel and any status into Gholdengo. **TWO HALVES, ONE ASSUMPTION-FREE**: the UNCONDITIONAL half fails on the USER's own state (Fake Out out of position, a Choice lock, no PP) and asserts nothing; the OCCUPANT-DEPENDENT half asserts *"they will not switch"*. Three of the four original examples are in the second half. The gate may still cut them — it is usually right and the expected cost is small in a format where the read is rare — but it is a PREDICTION and must be recorded as one. **DEFERRED BY WILL'S DECISION, NOT MISSED**: *"id rather not engage with those type of scenarios at the moment but make a note of it for things to possibly adjust much much later into the project."* When taken up, the home is not the gate: an action that is dead under one opponent action and live under another is a COLUMN of the equilibrium matrix, not a row to delete before coordination | deferred by decision — search; **NOT A DEFECT** (audited 2026-08-15: nothing here says a mechanic behaves wrongly — it says a SEARCH gate makes an unrecorded prediction. It was counted as an engine defect by the prose fallback, which matched the metaphor *"a Farigiraf is dead only while that Farigiraf is still there"*) |
| #253 | **THE WORD "ENDGAME" IS CLAIMED BY TWO MODELS IN OUR OWN LEDGER, AND THAT IS WHY THE ARCHITECTURE WAS HARD TO HOLD — WILL, 2026-08-13.** *"wait didnt we have dusknoir as the endgame guy? its hard to keep track."* He is right and the confusion is ours, not his memory. `docs/MODELS.md:1451` scopes **DUSK** as the endgame TABLEBASE — exact, precomputed offline, shipped as a lookup because it cannot be solved under a turn timer. SLOWKING's **Job:** line in the same file reads *"the endgame — tell you the equilibrium-best move (and win %) on a live position"*, which is not distinguishable in wording. **THE SPLIT, NOW WRITTEN DOWN**: DUSK is small endgames, EXACT, offline then looked up; SLOWKING is any position, approximate (an equilibrium over ESTIMATED values), live per turn; HYPNO is about the OPPONENT rather than the position and is a deliberate deviation FROM SLOWKING. **Only DUSK can say "guaranteed"** — which means Will's own description of the late-game stage (*"which ones lead to a guaranteed win... hand these scenarios to miltank"*) is DUSK's job and was filed under HYPNO. Fixed in `docs/MODELS.md` and `docs/ALAKAZAM-v2-spec.md`. **AND THE MATRIX IS KEPT EVEN IF GREEDY WINS**: *"id still want slowking matrix even if greedy wins right? because then if we play opponents regularly we can adapt and become unexploitable."* Keeping it is right and is already ADR-003's position — VGC-Bench's policy beats a Worlds competitor and is ~100% exploitable, so winning on average and being safe are different axes. **ONE CORRECTION TO THE REASONING**: adapting to an opponent makes you MORE exploitable, not less. Nash never adapts and cannot be beaten in the long run; deviating to punish a habit opens a hole in you. What repeated play actually buys is the other direction — a FIXED policy degrades because they learn YOU, and greedy is a fixed mapping. The matrix is not the adaptation mechanism; it is the floor you measure your deviations against, and HYPNO is the deviation | open — direction |
| #254 | **EVERY HAZARD LANDS ON THE SIDE THAT LAID IT IN THE OFFLINE BOARD, AND THE LIVE PATH IS RIGHT — SO THE FIT AND THE GAME DISAGREE. FOUND BY SEARCH 2026-08-13 WHILE CHASING #249; SPLIT OUT AND VERIFIED INDEPENDENTLY.** `engine/board.js` `noteMove` ends with `if (move.sideCondition) board.startSide(side, move.sideCondition, ...)` — `side` is the MOVER's, unconditionally. **DERIVED FROM THE FORMAT, NOT RECALLED: of the 11 legal side-condition moves in this regulation, SEVEN are `target: allySide` and FOUR are `target: foeSide`** — Stealth Rock, Spikes, Sticky Web, Toxic Spikes. The seven (Reflect, Light Screen, Aurora Veil, Safeguard, Tailwind, Quick Guard, Wide Guard) are correct BY ACCIDENT, because the mover's side is the right answer for them. The four hazards are recorded on the layer's own side, which is the side they can never be on. **THIS IS NOT #249 AND MUST NOT BE CLOSED WITH IT.** That row is the SEED carrying no hazards into a playout — a missing feature, and a rollout that ignores hazards is merely blind. This is a feature that is PRESENT AND INVERTED, and it sits in the board the FIT reads: `engine/board.js` is the offline board, the live path reads Showdown's `|-sidestart|` and gets it right. **That is FITTING ENVIRONMENT AND PLAYING ENVIRONMENT MUST MATCH, broken in a new place** — the same shape as MAG fitted with the sheet visible and played without it, and MACHAMP trained under broken mega. A model fitted on a board where Stealth Rock hurts its own team learned something about a game nobody plays. **SIZE, AND IT IS SMALL AND THAT IS NOT THE ARGUMENT**: 259 corpus uses across the four (Stealth Rock 168, Sticky Web 42, Toxic Spikes 34, Spikes 15) against 16,074 for Tailwind alone. A wrong value is worse than a missing one at equal volume, because nothing downstream can tell it is wrong. **THE FIX IS DERIVED, NOT A LIST OF FOUR**: read `move.target` and place the condition on the far side when it is `foeSide`. A hand-written hazard list here would be the ban-list-of-four shape a third time, and a hazard added next regulation would silently land on the wrong side again. **THREE SITES, NOT ONE, AND THEY ARE CURRENTLY WRONG TOGETHER — WHICH IS THE ONLY REASON NOTHING HAS BROKEN.** Read 2026-08-13 before anyone touches it: `engine/board.js:2675` WRITES with `board.startSide(side, ...)`; `:3311` READS with `board.hasSide(side, m.sideCondition)` to set `deadSide` ("this move cannot work, it is already up"); `:3343` READS the same way for `alreadyUp`, which gates `setupTurns`. All three take the mover's side, so today they are SELF-CONSISTENT and the error cancels — a hazard is written to the wrong side and then looked for on that same wrong side. **FIXING THE WRITE ALONE PRODUCES A WORSE BUG THAN THE ONE IT FIXES**: the hazard would land on the foe's side while both readers keep looking at the mover's, so `deadSide` would never fire for a hazard and the model would re-lay Stealth Rock every turn believing it was not up, crediting itself `setupTurns` each time. **SO IT IS ONE HELPER, NOT THREE EDITS** — a single `sideFor(board, side, move)` that answers "whose side does this condition live on" from `move.target`, called by the write and by both reads. Whose side a condition lands on is a FACT, and three call sites must not each decide it independently; that is the rule this repo has broken most expensively. `const foeSide = side === 'p1' ? 'p2' : 'p1'` is already an idiom at five other places in the same file, so the helper has a shape to match. **A PROBE MUST COVER BOTH DIRECTIONS**: a hazard laid by p1 is readable by p1 as "up on the far side" AND must still set `deadSide` when p1 tries to lay it again. A probe that only checks placement passes on an engine that placed it right and can no longer see it. **CLOSED 2026-08-13 BY MEASURE, AND THE ROW UNDERSTATED THE BLAST RADIUS BY SIX SITES.** `sideFor(side, move)` lives in `engine/board.js` beside the class, is exported, and is called by NINE sites: the write at `noteMove`, the two reads (`deadSide`, `alreadyUp`), and **six verbatim copies of the same mover's-side derivation found by ENGINE's sweep — `engine/fit_policy.js:793` (THE FIT ITSELF), `joint_rows.js`, `branch_recall.js`, `corpus_shift.js`, `feature_coverage.js`, `redirect_audit.js`.** Fixing only the three in `board.js` would have left the fit reading a board where a hazard hurts the team that laid it, which is the whole defect wearing a different file name. **ONE CORRECTION TO THIS ROW, MEASURED RATHER THAN REPEATED: "crediting itself `setupTurns` each time" IS WRONG.** None of the four hazards carries a `condition.duration` (Reflect 5, Tailwind 4, the hazards none), so `dur()` returns 0 and `setupTurns` is 0 for them under every variant. The observable movement is `deadSide` alone; the `alreadyUp` site is routed through the helper for the one-fact-one-function rule and binds today only on the seven `allySide` moves, where `sideFor` is the identity. **THE FINDING THAT OUTLIVES THE FIX IS THE GUARD'S.** `tests/test-feature-semantics.js` was STRUCTURALLY BLIND to this: run under both boards with the old fixture, **0 of 76 hashed columns moved**, because `engine/feature_fixture.js` contained **zero hazard clicks** and pre-set only `reflect` and `tailwind` — both `allySide`, exactly the half the fix leaves alone. A new board, `hazards-already-up`, puts `stealthrock`/`spikes` up on p2 and `stickyweb`/`toxicspikes` up on p1 so the flip is exercised in BOTH directions, and `deadSide` now moves `b984c210828d -> d1be4f95d589` with nothing else moving. **RED PROOFS, all three by compiling a pre-#254 `board.js` in memory and injecting it — the tree was never reverted:** `tests/test-hazard-side.js` 5 passed / 3 failed at HEAD and 11/0 after; the semantics guard's new clause names all six wrong candidates; and reverting ONE of the six sweep sites fails the ninth-site clause by file name. `tests/test-mechanics.js` **565 live / 0 missing**, unmoved, as expected — the whole-game differential exercises `medicham2`, not the feature board. **AND THE REFIT VERDICT IS NO, MEASURED: THE FIT IS BIT-IDENTICAL AND THIS WAS ONLY EVER A LIVE-PLAY DEFECT.** The whole fit corpus was replayed through `FP.decisionsFor` under both boards — **9,226 games, 237,052 decisions, 1,731,851 candidate vectors, 0 errored, identical decision key set — and 0 decisions, 0 games and 0 feature vectors move.** The reason is not thin exposure alone (24 hazard clicks: SR 19, Toxic Spikes 2, Sticky Web 2, Spikes 1; 258 decisions carry a hazard candidate) but an ISOMORPHISM, constructed rather than argued: `noteMove` wrote to the mover's side and both readers read the mover's side, so the old board is the new one with the two sides RELABELLED, and every offline-derived read agrees. **The mismatch is against a state that did NOT come from `noteMove` — which is the LIVE path.** `engine/magnemite.js:647` takes the side straight off `|-sidestart|` and calls `noteMove(..., worked=false)`, so on the pre-#254 board a hazard laid by p1 read **`deadSide` p1=0, p2=1** — the LAYER re-lays Stealth Rock forever believing it is not up, and the VICTIM refuses to lay its own believing it is. Fixed: p1=1, p2=0. Same probe, offline arm: identical under both boards for all 11 moves. **So MAG was fitted where `deadSide` was effectively right and PLAYED where it was backwards** — fitting-and-playing-environment-must-match broken in a new place, and closed in the one direction that costs no refit | **CLOSED 2026-08-13** |
| #255 | **GOOD AS GOLD REFUSES ALLY-AIMED STATUS MOVES IN THE AUTHORITY AND OURS ONLY REFUSES FOES — 2026-08-13, LEFT BY THE #241 PASS RATHER THAN HALF-FIXED.** The handler's condition is `move.category === "Status" && target !== source`, so it refuses anything that is not the holder ITSELF — including a partner's Decorate, Coaching, Skill Swap or Heal Pulse, measured on the authority. Every branch here is `_isFoe`-gated, so a Gholdengo accepts support from its own side that Showdown refuses. **THIS IS A REFUSAL DEFECT, NOT AN EMISSION ONE**, which is exactly why it was correct to leave it: #241 parts (1) and (2) were a NARRATION pass, and folding a behaviour change into them would have made a three-game differential move unattributable. **AND IT IS A REAL SET, NOT A CURIOSITY** — Gholdengo is the single carrier and ally-aimed support into your own Gholdengo is ordinary doubles play. Fix it with its own before/after. **CLOSED 2026-08-13, AND THE SWEEP DOUBLED THE SCOPE THE ROW STATED.** Derived first: one carrier in the regulation (Gholdengo, filtered walk) and `refusesStatusMoves` has EXACTLY ONE member over the whole tag corpus (`abilities:goodasgold`, 3,043 uses), printed before anything was wired. The authority was measured one battle per route — a supporter aiming at its own partner — and **26 routes all print `|-immune|p1b: Gholdengo|[from] ability: Good as Gold`**, including the untargetable ones (Life Dew, Howl, Heal Bell, Perish Song, Teeter Dance, Corrosive Gas); **NOT refused in the same sweep: Tailwind, Light Screen, Safeguard, Trick Room, Misty Terrain, Haze**, because a side and a field are not a body. Against that, 35 ally routes were played on this engine: **19 were already right and NINE were wrong in TWO shapes** — five branches gated the call on `_isFoe` (`boostally`, `reorder`, `abilityswap`, `abilitywrite`, `statrewire`; a partner's Skill Swap WALKED AWAY WITH GOOD AS GOLD) and four had **no refusal at all** (`helpinghand`, Life Dew's arm of `heal`, Howl's arm of `boostally`, and `perish`). **`perish` WAS THEREFORE WRONG FOR A FOE GHOLDENGO TOO** — no `_isFoe` audit could have found it, because there was no gate to audit. Fixed by making the one announcer SIDE-BLIND (`tryHitRefusal` already carried `t!==m`, which IS `target !== source`) and calling it PER BODY in the spread branches, so Howl still lifts the user and Perish Song still marks the other three; `pranksterBlocked` keeps its own foe gate inside that function because the authority gates it there, so this widens one ability and nothing else. New counter `MEDSEEN.tryHitRefusedAlly`. **THE PROBE WAS WRONG BEFORE THE ENGINE WAS, TWICE**: it read the `|move|` line as an effect (it names the target in field 4), and — the one that mattered — its exemption arm was a self-aimed Nasty Plot, which routes to `kind:'setup'`, **a branch that never asks about a refusal**, so deleting the `t===m` exemption outright left the probe GREEN. Every self route now goes through a branch this change edited. Red proof in both directions: exemption deleted -> `564/1` naming all four self routes; `_isFoe` restored on `boostally` alone -> `564/1` naming `decorate`, with the pre-existing foe-side Decorate probe still LIVE. Census **564 -> 565 live / 0 missing**. Whole-game differential, 1,220-game pinned pair, both arms, releases `0c5bb83c5744` -> `9b25e830df49` verified to differ in ONE file: top **254 -> 253**, bottom **294 -> 293**, `unrelated event mismatch` **30/26 -> 29/25** with every other class identical to the instance, and ONE cause gone by name — `|-immune|p2a|[from]goodasgold <> |-singleturn|p2a|helpinghand`, a real game where the authority refused a Helping Hand at a Gholdengo and we applied the mark. No new causes; normalised lines compared rose 1,507,925 -> 1,509,557, which is what removing an early stop looks like. **ONE GAME PER ARM IS THE INSTRUMENT'S REACH, NOT THE FIX'S SIZE** — the swarm aims 828 of ~46,700 clicks at an ally across 1,220 games and only the fraction landing on the one carrier can exercise this. `test-engine-diff.js --n 6000`: 6000 agreed, 0 disagreed, both corners | **CLOSED 2026-08-13** |
| #256 | **FOUR MORE onTryHit REFUSAL SITES ANSWER WRONGLY, NAMED RATHER THAN SWEPT — 2026-08-13.** Residue of #241, each written down instead of being folded into a pass that had already moved: **Role Play and Spite** reach `kind:'pass'`, which carries no target to announce a refusal against, so the refusal has nowhere to go; **Heal Pulse** emits a second `|-fail|<mover>` on the ORDINARY full-HP path, so it is wrong even when nothing refused it; **Soundproof and Overcoat** announce a bare `|-immune|` where the authority names the ability. The last one is the same shape as Magma Armor under #239 — an ability that refuses through a route the attribution reader does not cover — so the fix is to widen `immuneAttrIn()`'s reach rather than to add two names. **HOW THESE WERE FOUND IS THE PART TO KEEP**: a sweep over all 78 legal foe-aimed Status moves x 8 bodies, 624 cases, which is what turned "the refusal is already correct and only the line is wrong" into **twelve action kinds routing such a move and four distinct wrong answers**, with Lock-On and Heal Pulse carrying NO refusal at all — a Lock-On at a Gholdengo applied its guarantee and a Heal Pulse healed it. The row's own sizing was right and its scope was not; the sweep is the reason that was discovered before shipping rather than after. **CLOSED 2026-08-15, ALL FOUR SITES, EACH STAGED AGAINST THE AUTHORITY AND PRINTED SIDE BY SIDE BEFORE A LINE WAS EDITED.** (a) **ROLE PLAY AND SPITE** had TWO nowheres, not one: the terminal `{kind:'pass',mv:id}` dropped the target it was handed, so `TR.mv(m,_mid,_tt||m)` named the USER in field 4 *and* there was no body for a refusal to answer against — ours `|move|p1a: meowstic|roleplay|p1a: meowstic` and nothing after it, against `|move|p1a: Meowstic|Role Play|p2a: Gholdengo` + `|-immune|p2a: Gholdengo|[from] ability: Good as Gold`. Fixed with `mvFailAnnouncedByCaller`, a separate writer rather than a flag so a call site cannot silently lose the `|-fail|` it owes; `_mvRes` is deliberately UNCHANGED because `moveThisTurnResult` is cleared at the turn boundary and could not be staged off a finished battle. (c) **SOUNDPROOF AND OVERCOAT** — the row asked for `immuneAttrIn()` to be widened rather than for two names, and that is what landed: `immuneToMoveClass.announcesWith`, read off `onTryHit`/`onAllyTryHitSide` by the same reader #239 built for `onSetStatus`. **MEMBERSHIP PRINTED BEFORE IT WAS WIRED** — five carriers, four announcing (Overcoat, Soundproof, Bulletproof, Wind Rider) and one not (Magic Bounce, which reflects) — and **WIND RIDER HAS NO LEGAL CARRIER IN THIS REGULATION**, which is why its conditional `-immune` (it announces only when the +1 Attack fails) is a warning in the code and not a branch.
