| #259 | **ONE FROZEN BODY THAWS WITH AN ANNOUNCEMENT AND THE OTHER THAWS SILENTLY, ON THE SAME TURN — WILL, 2026-08-13, READING CARD 2.** *"for some reason we announce toxapex thaw but not incin thaw."* Both p1 bodies are frozen on turn 1; on turn 2 the authority emits `|-curestatus|p1a: Toxapex|frz|[msg]` AND `|-curestatus|p1b: Incineroar|frz|[msg]`. Ours emits the first and not the second, then plays Incineroar's Parting Shot normally — **so the thaw HAPPENS and only the line is missing.** **THE CODE READ DOES NOT EXPLAIN IT AND THAT IS WHY THIS IS A ROW RATHER THAN A FIX.** One branch handles both bodies — `if(m.status==='frz'){m.frzTurns=(m.frzTurns||0)+1; if(m.frzTurns>=3||rng()<0.25){m.status=''; if(TR)TR.cure(m,'frz',...);} else {...}}` — both enter with `frzTurns` 0, both draw the same pinned roll, both should reach `TR.cure`. Nothing in the branch distinguishes p1a from p1b. **THE SHAPE IS ONE THIS PROJECT HAS HISTORY WITH**: Toxapex is slot **a** and Incineroar is slot **b**, and *"mega evolution fired in self-play only from the LEFT slot"* is the same presentation. Suspicious, NOT asserted — reasoning from a code read was wrong twice on 2026-08-13 and this needs a staged two-frozen-bodies board before anyone edits. Reproduction is card 2 of `data/divergence-turns.json`, arm `bottom-tie-first`. **CLOSED 2026-08-15, AND THE MISSING LINE WAS THE SMALL HALF.** The row was right that the code read does not explain it: nothing in the freeze branch distinguishes p1a from p1b, because the difference is one block ABOVE, in what lets each body into the block at all. Staged on a two-frozen-bodies board against the authority with the thaw roll pinned LOSING: showdown `|cant|p1a: Toxapex|frz` AND `|cant|p1b: Incineroar|frz`; ours `|cant|p1a: toxapex|frz` then `|move|p1b: incineroar|partingshot|p2a: snorlax`. **THE FROZEN BODY MOVED.** The 2026-08-12 guard reads `it.a.kind==='switch'||it.a.kind==='pass'`, and a PIVOT MOVE is `{kind:'switch',mv}` — so Parting Shot skipped every `onBeforeMove` gate: sleep, freeze, flinch, confusion, Attract and the 12.5% full-paralysis roll, not just the thaw that exposed it. **THE SLOT-LETTER SUSPICION WAS WRONG AND THE FILE ALREADY OWNED THE RIGHT ANSWER**: `sdChoiceOf` says in as many words that a pivot carrying `mv` is a MOVE and Showdown queues it as choice `move`, so this was two answers to one question in one file — CLAUDE.md's facts-are-global rule — and the guard's own comment already said *a PASS AND A VOLUNTARY SWITCH*, with the word VOLUNTARY doing work the test never did. The guard now asks `actionMoveId(it.a)`, this file's one reader of *which move is this action*: a bare `{kind:'switch'}` (a real switch) and a bare `{kind:'pass'}` (a caller's do-nothing, which every probe in `tests/test-mechanics.js` hands in as PASS2) still skip. **MEMBERSHIP PRINTED OVER ALL 500 LEGAL MOVES BEFORE IT WAS WIRED**: `{kind:'switch',mv}` = chillyreception, partingshot; `{kind:'pass',mv}` = fairylock, healbell, roleplay, spite, teatime — SEVEN moves that were exempt from every status gate. The two redundant inline guards below it (confusion, Attract) moved to the same reader in the same pass, because leaving them would have kept Parting Shot exempt from those two and read exactly like a whole fix. Verified in both directions on the authority (roll pinned losing → `|cant|` on both bodies; pinned winning → `|-curestatus|...|frz|[msg]` on both) and on `spite` as well as `partingshot`. `tests/test-switch-carry.js` PART 3 — the sleeping body that pivots out, which is what put the guard there — is still 27/0. Census **579 → 584 live / 0 missing** across this row plus #241 and #256; probe `pivotStatus` asserts the OUTCOME (`[|move| lines, |switch| lines, |cant|frz lines]` = `[1,1,0]` unfrozen against `[0,0,1]` frozen, so a probe counting only announcements could not pass it), shown RED first in `tests/probe_red_demo.js` | **CLOSED 2026-08-15** |
| #260 | **"EVERY SECONDARY FIRES" SELF-CANCELS FOR ANY EFFECT WITH AN ESCAPE ROLL, SO THE BOTTOM ARM GUARANTEES FREEZE AND GUARANTEES THAW — WILL, 2026-08-13.** *"bro guaranteed freeze in the lower bound is so broken"*, then, one message later, *"but i guess we get guaranteed thaw?"* — both right, and together they name a property of the instrument nobody had stated. `makeArm` pins `random(den)` to **0** in the bottom arm, and `chance = (num, den) => random(den) < num`, so **every** `randomChance` returns true. The freeze secondary (`randomChance(1,10)`) fires AND the thaw check inside `frz.onBeforeMove` (`randomChance(1,4)`) also fires, so a body is frozen and cures on its very next move **without ever losing a turn**. The bottom arm therefore never exercises frozen-turn behaviour at all, while appearing to guarantee it. **THE EFFECT IS NOT UNIFORM AND THAT IS THE POINT**: an effect with ONE roll compounds (paralysis is applied AND fully paralyses every turn; confusion is applied AND self-hits), an effect with an ESCAPE roll cancels (freeze). *"The worst corner"* is therefore the wrong mental model for this arm — it is *"every branch taken"*, which is the worst case only when there is one branch. **NOT A DEFECT IN THE PIN**, which is doing exactly what it says; it is a COVERAGE claim that needs narrowing, and the arm's own description (*"every secondary fires"*) is what misleads. **AND A LATENT COLLISION FOUND WHILE CHECKING IT**: `random(m)` returns the damage index when `m === DAMAGE_ROLL_SIDES` (16), so any `randomChance(x, 16)` would be read as a damage roll rather than a chance. Derived: **no legal move in this regulation has a 1-in-16 secondary**, so it is latent today and would surface silently the day one is added | open — measure |
| #261 | **THE GAME LETS A FROZEN BODY THAW ITSELF BY ATTACKING AND THIS ENGINE DOES NOT, BECAUSE `flags.defrost` HAS NO REPRESENTATION ANYWHERE HERE — WILL, 2026-08-13.** *"we need to test that the moves that auto thaw you out (either by the user or getting hit by it) actually thaw."* He named both routes and **the format has both while this engine has one.** Derived, filtered to the regulation: **`flags.defrost` — the USER thaws when it USES the move — has five members: Burn Up, Flare Blitz, Matcha Gotcha, Scald, Scorching Sands.** `thawsTarget` — the TARGET thaws on being HIT — has **three**: Matcha Gotcha, Scald, Scorching Sands. They are different rules over overlapping sets. **`grep -c defrost` RETURNS 0 IN BOTH `engine/medicham2-browser.js` AND `data/tags.json`.** The authority's `frz.onBeforeMove` opens `if (move.flags["defrost"] && !(move.id === "burnup" && !pokemon.hasType("Fire"))) return;` — the frozen body simply moves. Ours has no such clause: the thaw branch reads only `m.frzTurns>=3||rng()<0.25`, so a frozen Incineroar clicking Flare Blitz waits on the 25% roll instead of thawing and attacking. **AND THE TAG WE DO HAVE IS A COLLAPSED PAIR, WHICH IS TASK #1'S SHAPE A SECOND TIME**: `data/tags.json` tags **all five** as `thawsTarget`, so the label is wrong about two of its own members. Burn Up and Flare Blitz do NOT thaw a target — they defrost their user — and that is **masked today because both are Fire and `frz.onDamagingHit` cures on ANY Fire hit** (`move.type === "Fire" && move.category !== "Status" && move.id !== "polarflare"`), so the right thing happens for the wrong reason and no test can tell. Only the three non-Fire members need a `thawsTarget` tag at all; the Fire ones are covered by type. **THE TITLE OF THIS ROW WAS ORIGINALLY A CLAIM ABOUT POKEMON RATHER THAN ABOUT US, AND WILL READ IT THAT WAY** (2026-08-13: *"no frozen bodies can thaw itself if they use certain moves"* — correcting what he took to be a claim that the mechanic does not exist). It does exist, in five moves; what is missing is OUR half. A row heading that cannot be told apart from a statement about the game is the same hazard CLAUDE.md names for illegal entities, one level up, and it cost a round trip here. **THE FIX IS A SPLIT, NOT AN ADDITION**: a `thawsUser` tag parsed off `flags.defrost` with the Burn-Up-needs-Fire-type exception carried as a param, and `thawsTarget` narrowed to the moves that actually do it. **PROBE BOTH ROUTES AND THE FIRE CONFOUND**: a frozen user clicking Flare Blitz (thaws, no tag needed for the target), a frozen target hit by Scald (thaws, non-Fire, needs the tag), and a frozen target hit by a Fire move with neither flag (thaws by type alone) — without the third arm a probe cannot tell the type route from the tag route. **DONE, 2026-08-13.** Census **567 -> 570 live / 0 missing**. `thawsUser` parsed off `flags.defrost` (5 members) carrying `requiresUserType` read from Burn Up's own `onTryMove` through the SAME reader `spendsOwnType.requires` uses — one implementation, two callers — and `thawsTarget` narrowed to `m.thawsTarget` (3 members). The engine clause sits ABOVE the counter tick because the authority's `return` spends no turn and draws no die. Three probes in `tests/test-mechanics.js`, two of them shown MISSING against the shipped engine first; four green-then-red demonstrations in `tests/probe_red_demo.js`, including the target route's TYPE clause, which nothing had ever shown red on its own. Whole-game differential `b4746a1e44a7 -> 652b971cb70d`, 978 games: no count moved in any arm, and the class `-curestatus field 4` DISAPPEARS from the bottom corner — the attribution now reads `[from] move: flareblitz` where it wrote `[msg]`. `test-engine-diff.js --n 6000`: 0/6000 both corners | done — `docs/ENGINE.md`, `docs/MEDICHAM-SPRINT-NOTES.md` |
| #262 | **A MIDDLE ARM WITH EVENT-ADDRESSED DICE, BECAUSE BOTH PINNED CORNERS PRODUCE GAMES NOBODY PLAYS — WILL, 2026-08-13.** *"is it possible at all for us to run a middle bound? where things can miss, and secondary chances have a chance to proc? to get some more realistic scenarios to test... otherwise paralysis ends the mons usefulness... thats why games take years."* **He is right about the mechanism and it is measurable**: the bottom arm pins every `randomChance` true (see #260), so the full-paralysis roll fires EVERY turn and a paralysed body never moves again — which is a large part of why the median game hits the 12-turn cap when a real one ends at **7**. **WHY RANDOM DICE ARE NOT THE ANSWER, AND THIS IS THE WHOLE DESIGN PROBLEM**: the arms are pinned for SYNCHRONISATION, not caution. Both engines must give the same answer to the same question or a divergence is a dice disagreement rather than a rule disagreement, and they make different numbers of draws in different orders, so a shared seeded stream desynchronises on turn one. A constant is the only mapping that survives that. **THE WAY OUT IS TO ADDRESS THE DIE BY THE EVENT RATHER THAN BY POSITION IN A SEQUENCE**: `hash(turn, attacker slot, move id, effect name) % 100 < chance`. Order-independent, so the engines agree without being in lockstep; some Rock Slides miss and some flinch; paralysis fires near 1-in-4 instead of every turn; games END. Across thousands of events the observed rates approach the printed ones, which also makes it the natural home for the rate work in #196/#28. **THE COST, STATED**: the two engines must NAME the event identically — same move id, same turn, same target — or the hashes differ and every roll disagrees. The normaliser already maps ids, so it is checkable, and it must be ASSERTED rather than assumed: a probe that plants a known event on both sides and compares the drawn value. **AND IT INHERITS #260**: an effect with an escape roll must not get "true" for both the apply and the escape, which a hash gives for free and a constant never can | **ENGINE HALF LANDED 2026-08-13 — the engine names the event; the AUTHORITY half is still unwired and measures 0.0%.** medicham2 now carries a module-level event address written at four sites (top of the turn, top of the action, the announcement, the step driver) and `midEventDice`/`midEventLog` export it; `tests/test-middle-identity.js` DIFFS the two engines' address logs over 900 real games. Measured, medicham2-matched: **acc 99.7%, dmg 98.8%, crit 98.5%, sec 91.0% — 98.3% pooled over 1,058 events**, floored per category (pooling hid a break: deleting the per-target write left the pool at 91.2% over a floor of 90). Four deliberate breaks, three RED, and the fourth GREEN is itself the finding — the announcement-site write is redundant over 900 games and is now COUNTED rather than trusted. **The row cannot close on ENGINE's side alone.** `game_differential.js` builds its address inside the function it installs as `battle.prng.random`, and `Battle#random` is `this.prng.random(m,n)` — so `this` there is the PRNG and every authority address is `<seed>|undefined|<cat>|-|-|<nth>`. The test computes BOTH streams on every draw and asserts the degenerate one stays under 5%, so the arm cannot be switched on by mistake. Owed in that file: capture `this.battle` in `midWrapShowdown`'s `around()`, and clear `MID_NTH` per game beside the existing `midReset()`. **Three category mislabels fell out of the diff by counting, one of them a standing ROADMAP #222 gap**: the move's own secondaries loop was on the generic stream (28 authority `sec` draws against ZERO from us), and King's Rock and Dire Claw likewise. **What is NOT solved and is not claimed**: `nth` is a repeat counter, so a differing draw count at one address still parts after entry 0 — 457 of 2,029 events are `nth > 0` and that is printed every run | open — measure (authority half) |
| #263 | **THE STORE HAS NO SPREADS AT ALL, SO A REPLAY DIFFERENTIAL CANNOT ADJUDICATE DAMAGE OR SPEED — WILL, 2026-08-13, AND HE TALKED ME OUT OF LEADING WITH IT.** I proposed running `engine/replay_differential.js` against real games as the realistic instrument. *"we dont know the sps on games that actually happened tho"*, then *"so the damage and speed would be off."* **Measured on `data/games.bo3.jsonl`: 4,001 games, 47,856 sheet bodies, EVERY ONE `"evs": null`, ZERO with a real spread.** An open team sheet shows species, ability, item, moves and nature — never the stat points. **SPEED IS THE WORSE HALF AND THAT IS THE PART I MISSED**: damage being off produces a wrong number, but speed being off produces a wrong TURN ORDER, which is categorical — every line after it diverges for a reason that is not a rule. `replay_differential.js`'s own header already makes this argument about reconstructing CHOICES (*"a reconstruction that guesses is worse than no test, because a guess that happens to be wrong looks exactly like an engine defect"*) and does not apply it to the spreads. **THE DISTINCTION THAT MATTERS IS CONTROL, NOT REALISM**: the synthetic differential's spreads are unknown AND IDENTICAL ON BOTH SIDES, so a divergence can only be a rule; a replay's spreads are unknown AND DIFFERENT from whatever the player actually brought, so a divergence may be either. **Unknown-but-identical is fine; unknown-and-wrong is not.** So the replay differential keeps a NARROWER job — sequences and categorical facts independent of exact numbers (did that ability fire, did that switch happen, was that move legal) — and cannot arbitrate a KO or a speed tie, which is most of what a game turns on. This is ROADMAP #68 restated with its consequence: it is NARROWED by the nature and can never be closed by it. **Do not lead with replay; build #262 instead**, which is synthetic and therefore controls its own spreads | open — measure |
| #264 | **A 100-ACCURACY MOVE CAN MISS, AND THE DIFFERENTIAL ASSERTS THAT IT CANNOT — WILL, 2026-08-13.** *"i mean even if a move is 100 accuracy it could still miss due to evasion, bright powder, sand veil, etc."* **He is right and the population is larger than the three he named.** Derived and filtered to the regulation: **3 items** (Bright Powder, Wide Lens, Zoom Lens), **6 abilities** (Sand Veil, Snow Cloak, Compound Eyes, Hustle, Tangled Feet, Wonder Skin) and **3 moves** (Double Team, Minimize, Sweet Scent) touch accuracy or evasion. The authority keeps `accuracy === true` (cannot miss) strictly apart from `accuracy === 100` (rolls, after boosts and modifiers): `hitStepAccuracy` runs the `Accuracy` event and the evasion stages ONLY when it is not `true`, so a printed-100 move into a +2 evasion body is a **60% move**. **WHAT IS CONFIRMED HERE**: `hitChance` does the stage arithmetic, WIRE 129 fixed the bug where `accuracy` and `evasion` mapped to NULL so every Double Team silently vanished, `evasionIgnored` is counted, and the one place a 100 becomes never-miss is documented and CORRECT — rain Thunder, where Showdown itself writes `move.accuracy = true` and the artifact lossily records 100. **WHAT IS NOT CONFIRMED, AND THAT IS THE ROW**: that a printed-100 move into a boosted-evasion body actually misses HERE, at the right rate. The mechanism being present is not evidence it fires — this project's founding lesson — and no probe covers it. **AND THE CLAIM IS LOAD-BEARING RATHER THAN COSMETIC**: `game_differential.js` asserts *"a 100-accuracy move HITS"* as a PIN CLAIM on both corners. If evasion is live, that claim is false whenever a Bright Powder or a Sand Veil is on the field, and the arm would be asserting something the game does not do. **PROBE SHAPE**: a printed-100 move into a body at +0 evasion (never misses), the same into +2 (misses ~40% of the time), and the same into a Bright Powder holder — three arms, because two cannot separate the stage arithmetic from the item modifier **CLOSED 2026-08-13 — AND HALF THE ROW WAS ALREADY TRUE, WHICH IS WHY MEASURING CAME FIRST.** The OUTCOME was never wrong: `hitChance` has always returned the FINAL accuracy and the roll sites have always gated on that, so a printed-100 Ice Beam into a +2 body missed 38.3% here against the authority's 38.8%, and into a Bright Powder holder 9.7% against 10.3%. What was missing was the PROBE, and there are now two — a three-armed RATE probe (a pinned roll cannot tell 60 from 99, and the stage route and the item route are different code) plus an Aerial Ace control that must also be shown LANDING. **THE REAL DEFECT WAS THE DRAW**, wrong in both directions: the attack and stat-change sites gated on `acc < 100`, so a Coil or a Wide Lens (final 133 / 110) stopped drawing where the authority draws, and the two STATUS sites drew unconditionally including on the Infinity a Poison-type Toxic produces, where the authority draws nothing. `accMustRoll(acc)` is now one answer for all four sites and it is `isFinite(acc)`. **THE AMENDMENT'S CHEAP PREDICATE WAS BUILT AND IS A MEASURED REGRESSION** — middle-arm VOID 131/171 before, **133** with the predicate (worse than doing nothing, because it took draws away from the status sites), **100** with the authority's rule; usable games 40 -> 71. And the speed premise does not survive measurement: the skip never avoided the pipeline (`hitChance` runs unconditionally and always did — the guard sat underneath it), and three interleaved rounds of 6,000 turns put the three variants inside each other's round-to-round spread. Census **565 -> 567 live / 0 missing**; four deliberate breaks shown RED. The corners cannot see this and that is now measured rather than suspected: two frozen releases differing in exactly one file, 200 games, top 53/171 and bottom 59/171 **before and after**. `game_differential.js` PIN_CLAIMS need a re-wording (they say "a 100-accuracy move HITS" and assert a FINAL accuracy of 100) — reported, not edited, since MEASURE owns that file. | closed 2026-08-13 — `docs/ENGINE.md` |
| #265 | **FIXTURES WERE DECLARING SETS THE GAME WOULD REFUSE, AND NOTHING IN THE REPOSITORY ASKED — CLOSED 2026-08-13 BY MEASURE.** CLAUDE.md's cardinal rule is that no Pokémon, item, ability or move outside the regulation may be NAMED, in *"every example, every illustration and every derived result"* — and it was being broken inside the tests, where it is worse than in prose: a fixture built on a set that cannot exist measures a game we do not play, and every number it produces inherits that. **BOTH KNOWN INSTANCES WERE FOUND IN PASSING RATHER THAN BY A CHECK**, which is the argument: `engine/feature_fixture.js` gave Venusaur a ROCKY HELMET (banned here since 2026-08-04) and it was spotted by an agent doing #254; seven illegal learnset pairs reached `data/fixture-learnset-baseline.json` because an agent sent to repair one faint happened to run the validator. **THE VERDICT IS `TeamValidator#validateTeam`'s AND NOTHING RE-IMPLEMENTS IT.** Will: *"i thought we were using showdowns team validator as the ultimate legality test."* The first plan was species `isNonstandard`, then the ability against the species list, then the item, then `checkCanLearn` per move — four hand-written rules standing in for a table Showdown maintains, which is FACTS ARE GLOBAL broken inside the file written to enforce a rule. A piecemeal check only finds the rules somebody thought to write; the validator carries the 66-point budget, the 32-per-stat cap, the item clause and every ban the mod adds next. `engine/fixture_legality.js` therefore calls `champions_sim.checkLegal` — one shared validator instance, five proved-clean padding slots — and prints the authority's own sentences. **THE POPULATION, DERIVED RATHER THAN LISTED: 334 .js files, 627 set declarations in 26 files, 227 distinct sets, 55 rejected, 41 distinct verdicts.** Four scanner rules each went in after it was caught being wrong on a real file: a literal counts as an entity only if it normalises to that entity's own id (`dex.species.get('p2')` answers Porygon2 and `p2` here is a SIDE); moves come only from ARRAY literals (`hit('vaporeon','icebeam')` clicks the move at that body, not from it — three false accusations); helpers are taken at TOP LEVEL only (`test-mechanics.js` redeclares `run`/`hit`/`at` in dozens of probe callbacks and a file-global name set paired an ability stamped on the ATTACKER with the DEFENDER); and a set is declared either as an object literal or by MUTATION, which is 64 real sets in `test-protocol-trace.js`. **507 construction sites carry no literal and are outside the population, correctly** — a fixture that derives its bodies from the format cannot type a name that does not exist. **THE GATE IS `tests/test-fixture-legality.js`, ratcheted on `data/fixture-legality-baseline.json`, keyed on the VALIDATOR'S OWN SENTENCE** so the same illegal declaration moved to a new scenario is the same defect. Six clauses: a population floor (a scanner that finds nothing passes everything below it), no new verdict, the baseline may only SHRINK, every entry states a kind and a written reason, a stray literal that names nothing in the format (`'dampro'` for Damp Rock at `test-protocol-trace.js:131` — the item resolves to nothing and the fixture silently runs without it), and a self-check that the ratchet still discriminates. **SHOWN RED THREE WAYS**: re-planting the real Rocky Helmet fails naming `engine/feature_fixture.js:87`; two invented baseline entries fire the stale-allowance clause; one with a bad kind fires the reason clause. **SIX REPAIRS, ALL IN MEASURE'S OWN FIXTURE, COVERAGE MEASURED ACROSS EVERY ONE**: Rocky Helmet → Miracle Seed (4 sites), Electric Seed → Mental Herb, Throat Spray → White Herb, Grimmsnarl's Thunder Wave → Taunt, and Hippowdon's WEATHER BALL, which no legal sand or Rock body in this regulation learns — the flip moved to a new board `sand-weather-ball` rather than displacing anything, after an in-place Heliolisk swap was tried and MEASURED to thin `allyHit` 4→3 and `abilityBlock` 3→2 while going silent nowhere. **AND THE GUARD'S OWN IDENTITY CHECK WAS THE FINDING THAT OUTLIVES THE FIX**: `verify()` decided *"is this the same fixture?"* from ROUND and the scenario LABELS, so editing a body inside a scenario that keeps its name reported every moved column as *"these features changed MEANING"* — falsely accusing `board.js` of a change it did not make, and collapsing the refit/restamp distinction the whole file turns on. It now carries a BODY digest, backward-compatible with older stamps | closed 2026-08-13 — `docs/MEASURE.md` |
| #266 | **TWENTY-TWO ILLEGAL FIXTURE VERDICTS AND TWENTY-THREE DECLARATIONS REMAIN, IN FOUR FILES MEASURE DOES NOT OWN — THE ROW WAS SHRUNK TO ITS REMAINDER 2026-08-15 AND THE GATE IS GREEN.** **THE HEADLINE SAID FORTY-ONE FOR TWO DAYS AFTER IT STOPPED BEING TRUE**, which is the thing this register keeps doing and the thing that costs an agent: `node tests/test-fixture-legality.js` exits **0** today over 348 files / 639 set declarations / 232 distinct sets, with every one of the 22 verdicts and 23 declarations on the baseline and no stale allowance. **AND IT IS NOT AN ENGINE DEFECT AT ALL** — it is fixture hygiene; it held the MEDICHAM gate shut only because the gate's prose fallback matched the words *"a row that does not exist"* two thousand characters into this row's own account of a fixture bug it had already repaired. **THE ORIGINAL ROW, KEPT WHOLE:** **FORTY-ONE ILLEGAL FIXTURE DECLARATIONS ARE BASELINED AND OWED A REPAIR BATCH — OPENED 2026-08-13 BY #265.** They are not MEASURE's files and each repair changes what its scenario measures, so folding forty into the pass that ADDS the check makes both unattributable — the same reasoning already recorded in `data/fixture-learnset-baseline.json`, of which this baseline is the superset. **BY FILE — verdicts touching each, which SUM ABOVE 41 because one sentence can be produced in several files (Snorlax|Swords Dance in eleven places across two): `tests/staged_board.js` 15, `tests/test-protocol-trace.js` 12** (several are mainline recollections the mod does not carry — *"Gengar can't have Levitate"*, Champions gives it Cursed Body; plus the `'dampro'` typo), **`tests/staged_status_counters.js` 7, `tests/test-perish-song.js` 3, `tests/test-volatile-duration.js` 3, `tests/test-game-diff.js` 2, `tests/test-switch-carry.js` 2, `tests/test-click-censoring.js` 2**, and one each in `test-speed-tie.js`, `test-nature-differential.js`, `probe_volatile_leaves.js`. **THE COST OF EACH IS THE SAME SHAPE**: the body was chosen for a typing, a bulk, a speed or a pairing with another test, and a legal replacement moves the board rather than renaming it — `test-perish-song.js` and `test-volatile-duration.js` deliberately share bodies so a duration change cannot pass in one and fail in the other, and `test-speed-tie.js` is pinned to four 100-base-Speed bodies. **NOTHING MAY BE RE-BASELINED INTO SILENCE**: the gate fails on a baselined verdict that stops being produced, so a repair must remove its entry. **PART ONE DONE 2026-08-14 (MEASURE): 41 verdicts → 32, 55 rejected sets → 45, the one stray literal → 0, gate ALL GREEN on the shrunk baseline with the population unchanged at 627/227.** Ten came off, each named with its cost in the new `repaired` block of `data/fixture-legality-baseline.json`. **The stray was the worst and no validator could ever have reported it**: `'dampro'` resolves to a row that does not exist whose `.name` is `''`, and an empty item is a LEGAL item, so the fixture built a Politoed HOLDING NOTHING and everything went green. **Both red gates' fixture audits are green and `tests/test-perish-song.js` is green outright**; the singer is AZUMARILL and the Taunt/Encore/Disable user is ALAKAZAM, both DERIVED (six legal bodies learn Perish Song+Protect, seven learn Taunt+Encore+Disable) and the cost is named: **none of the seven can have Pressure**, so the doubled-PP rate is no longer staged in that file. **`tests/test-volatile-duration.js` still parts from Showdown on HP and that is NOT the repair — measured, not assumed**: the pre-repair fixture was replayed and parts in all four scenarios by the same magnitude and direction. Its absolute verdict is WITHHELD because `engine/medicham2-browser.js` was being written at 02:26 during the runs; only the PAIRED claim is published. **PART TWO, 2026-08-15 (MEASURE). 32 verdicts to 22; the REAL population was never 32 and is now 23.** **THE RULER WAS SHORT**: `validateTeam` returns ONE complaint per Pokemon, so a Snorlax carrying four moves it cannot learn produced one sentence, and repairing the first made the second read as a NEW verdict. Every declared move now also goes through `checkCanLearn`, and the baseline ratchets those PAIRS beside the sentences — 34 before this batch, 23 after. **THE CLASSES ARE THREE AND ONLY ONE IS EXPENSIVE**: 0 illegal SPECIES, 33 re-aimable pairings, and **1 UNREACHABLE — Spore, which has ZERO legal carriers in this regulation**, derived twice (whole-roster `checkCanLearn`, and an independent raw learnset walk through prevo and base-forme chains; both answer 0). `tests/staged_status_counters.js` stages BOTH sleep-counter scenarios on it, and **the repair is not a rename**: every clickable sleep move here is sub-100 accuracy (Hypnosis 60, Sleep Powder 75, Sing 55) and both scenarios run on `top-tie-first`, where every sub-100 move MISSES by construction. Milotic can legally learn Hypnosis, so the mechanic exists on the bottom arm and nowhere else — HELD, filed to the owner of the differential, and declared `unreachable: true` so it can never be read as an engine defect. **REPAIRED: 10 verdicts / 11 declarations**, all in `tests/test-protocol-trace.js` and `tests/test-click-censoring.js`, each onto a DERIVED legal carrier with its cost named — Rotom carries the Levitate this format never let Gengar have (its `abilities` is `{"0":"Cursed Body"}`), Reuniclus splits at Snorlax's exact 30 Speed, Mamoswine phazes, Meganium sets the terrain and throws the Hyper Beam, and Thief replaces Knock Off in the PART 5 acceptance case that `new Battle()` never validated. All 41 claimed protocol events still fire. **A POPULATION HOLE, MEASURED AND CLOSED**: a set written as a positional row `['species',[moves],ability,item]` was neither of the sweep's two shapes, and twelve such rows drive 200 games — **five were illegal, including a Toxapex holding an item that does not exist in Gen 9**. Repaired first, matcher added after: 627 to 639 declarations, verdict count unmoved. **THE BASELINE COULD BE LAUNDERED AND NOW CANNOT**: `PRE-EXISTING` is a claim about TIME and nothing checked it, so 'repair one' and 'excuse a new one' were the same edit. The 41 historical sentences are now a closed `origin` set; a PRE-EXISTING entry outside it fails by name, and an UNREACHABLE entity may never be called DELIBERATE. Shown RED on the full attempt — offender planted AND added to `verdicts` AND to `pairs` AND marked `unreachable`, exit 1. **Still open: 22 verdicts / 23 declarations**, all in files MEASURE does not own: `tests/staged_board.js` 16, `tests/staged_status_counters.js` 7, `tests/test-switch-carry.js` 2, `tests/test-click-censoring.js` 1 — the last being Farigiraf/Encore, where **no legal body in this regulation learns Encore, Roar and Follow Me together** and that fixture's one body plays all three roles. **NO `VERIFIED BY` MARKER, AND THE REASON IS THE MARKER'S OWN LIMIT**: `tests/test-fixture-legality.js` is a RATCHET with a baseline, so it is GREEN today with all 22 verdicts standing — its exit code says *no NEW illegal fixture*, never *the debt is gone*. Marking it would make `engine/register_reality.js` report this row STALE while 22 declarations sit in the baseline, which is the same false confidence one level up. A marker is only honest where the instrument's green means the row's claim is FALSE. | open — fixture legality debt, 22 verdicts / 23 declarations, gate GREEN; **not an engine defect** |
| #267 | **A STATUS IS SEEDED AND THE COUNTER HANGING OFF IT IS NOT, SO SLEEP RESTARTS AND TOXIC RESETS — FOUND BY SEARCH 2026-08-13 WHILE CLOSING #248.** The seed now carries `status` onto every body, active and benched, and carries NOTHING about how far through that status the body is. `buildMon` returns `slp: 0` and the toxic ramp starts at stage 0, so a body two turns into a one-or-two-turn sleep wakes late in every playout, and a `tox` at stage 5 chips at stage 1. **THE ENGINE IS NOT THE PROBLEM AND ALREADY HAS THE HARDER HALF RIGHT**: medicham2's 2026-08-12 note walks the format's own condition table and records that `par/brn/psn/frz/slp` have no switch handler so their counters CARRY OVER, while `tox.onSwitchIn` sets `stage = 0` so the ramp RESTARTS — it models both. **THE BOARD RECORDS ONLY THE STATUS NAME.** Both worlds can supply the counter: the protocol's `|-status|` gives the turn it landed and the store's `x` event does too, so the quantity is `turn - turnItLanded`, derived once on the board rather than a second counter per consumer. Sizing: `x` events appear in the bo3 store at scale and every one of them is a body whose status clock the seed sets to zero. **CLOSED 2026-08-14 BY SEARCH.** The quantity is derived ONCE, on the board: `board.statusClock` is a per-side, per-SPECIES ledger — the same shape `pp` is and for the same reason, because the counter belongs to the Pokemon and `switchIn` builds a new object every time a body comes out — booked in `endTurn` for every body that is ON THE FIELD and carrying a status, which is not a simplification but the engine's own rule (`slpTurns` moves when the body ACTS, so a sleeper that pivots out keeps its count and does not deepen). `rollout_leaf.seedStatusClock` writes it into `slpTurns`/`toxTurns`/`frzTurns`; `par` and `brn` get nothing because nothing about them changes with time, and `tox` is written even for a benched body so the engine can apply its own `toxStageResetOnEntry` rather than a second copy of that rule living here. **NO CALLER CHANGED AND NO REFIT IS OWED**: the ledger is written by `endTurn` alone, so the live adapter, the fitter's replay and every fixture stamp it without being edited, and no FEATURE reads it. **Off by at most one and the direction is stated**: a body slept AFTER it had already moved reads one turn light, which errs by keeping it asleep marginally longer. Prevalence, over 14,288 open-sheet games / 192,912 decision points: **8.243% of decision points have a body some turns into slp/tox/frz, 7.405% of them on the field** — a CEILING, because the store records a landing and never a cure. **VERIFIED BY: `node tests/test-seed-clock.js`** — the register row and the gate that decides it, checked against each other by `engine/register_reality.js` rather than by anyone remembering. | closed 2026-08-14 — SEARCH; gate tests/test-seed-clock.js; artifact data/rollout-clock-prevalence.json |
| #268 | **THE BOARD GIVES A PERMANENT HAZARD A DURATION AND COUNTS NO LAYERS, SO THE SEED CAN ONLY SEE IT BRIEFLY AND ONLY ONE DEEP — FOUND BY SEARCH 2026-08-13 WHILE CLOSING #249.** Derived from the format, not recalled: Stealth Rock, Spikes, Sticky Web and Toxic Spikes carry NO `condition.duration`, because they last until they are removed. `board.startSide(side, cond, duration)` defaults an absent duration to `turn + 1`, so in the OFFLINE path — the fitter's board — a hazard is up for exactly one turn; the live path passes `fieldDuration`'s default of 5 and is wrong for longer. Measured on a fixture: laid `true`, one `endTurn()` later `false`, while the real rocks are still there. **SO `deadSide` GOES BACK TO ZERO AND THE MODEL RE-LAYS STEALTH ROCK**, which is the same symptom #254 measured for the write-only variant and the reason that row insists the write and the reads move together. Layers are the second half: `sideConditions` is a Map of expiry, not a count, so a seeded Spikes or Toxic Spikes is always ONE layer and the difference between one and two Toxic Spikes is poisoned against badly poisoned. **THIS IS NOT SEARCH'S**: fixing it moves `deadSide` and `setupTurns`, which are fitted FEATURES, so it invalidates `data/policy-weights.json` and belongs with the refit. `tests/test-rollout-seed.js` §6 prints the fixture's before/after on every run so the day it closes is visible. **CLOSED 2026-08-14 BY SEARCH — AND THE "FIT-INVALIDATING" CLAIM IS CORRECTED, MEASURED RATHER THAN ARGUED.** It is fit-AFFECTING in kind and immaterial in size: over the fitter's own replay of **800 human games / 139,340 candidates scored**, `deadSide` fires **927 times with the fix and 925 without it — a difference of TWO candidates** — and no other feature moves at all (the whole `feature_coverage` table and `feature_fixture`'s 58 per-feature column hashes are byte-identical). The reason is the format rather than the code: **60 hazard clicks across 14,288 open-sheet games**, and not one hazard is laid in the first 120 games of the fit corpus. `setupTurns` does not move at all, exactly as #254's own note predicted — no hazard carries a `condition.duration`, so `dur()` is 0 whatever `alreadyUp` says. **THE FIX IS ONE FUNCTION AND IT ASKS THE FORMAT, NOT THE CALLER**: `startSide` now decides permanence itself — the same shape `sideFor` uses one screen down, because whose side a condition lands on and how long it lasts are both FACTS — from a set derived in `derived()` (a move declaring no `condition.duration`) with the `hazard` tag as a dex-free fallback, so the live adapter's guessed `fieldDuration` of 5 is overruled without magnemite.js being touched. The FIRST version read only `derived()` and was MEASURED WRONG on a board that had never scored a feature — four Spikes read one layer deep, the defect wearing the shape of the fix — which is why the tag path exists and why `sideCounters` counts every fallback. **LAYERS ARE THE SECOND HALF AND ARE COUNTED NOW**: the Map's value is `{until, layers}`, clamped at the tag's own `maxLayers`, and `board.sideLayers` is the one reader; `applySideState`'s `Math.min(1, …)` — a constant 1 — is gone, so three Spikes take 1/4 of a switch-in rather than 1/8 (asserted behaviourally: −17 HP at one layer, −35 at three) and Toxic Spikes can reach the second layer that makes a body BADLY poisoned. Prevalence: **a hazard is up at 0.271% of decision points, 0.08% of them older than five turns, 0.015% more than one layer deep** — real, correct, and very nearly inert, which is stated rather than dressed up. **VERIFIED BY: `node tests/test-seed-clock.js`** — the register row and the gate that decides it, checked against each other by `engine/register_reality.js` rather than by anyone remembering. | closed 2026-08-14 — SEARCH; gate tests/test-seed-clock.js; artifact data/rollout-clock-prevalence.json |

| #270 | **THE SEEDED FIELD HAS NO CLOCK, SO A WEATHER WITH ONE TURN LEFT LASTS THE WHOLE PLAYOUT — FOUND BY SEARCH 2026-08-13 WHILE SWEEPING THE SEED.** `applyField` sets `S.field.weather` and `S.field.terrain` and never `weatherT`/`terrainT`, and the engine's tick is `if (field.weatherT > 0 && --field.weatherT <= 0)` — so **ZERO MEANS NEVER EXPIRES**. the WEATHER clock is not (`setWeather` takes no duration and records none), so the board must first learn it — from `|-weather|` plus the dex duration plus the holder's rock, which is exactly what `weatherTurns` already computes. **DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED IN THE #247–#250 BATCH**: it is one fact, half of it needs board work, and landing half of it inside a four-row batch is what makes the batch's own result unattributable. **CLOSED 2026-08-14 BY SEARCH, AND IT IS THE BIGGEST OF THE FOUR BY A WIDE MARGIN: a weather is up at 32.223% of decision points, with a mean of 2.95 turns left, and the seed ran every one of them for the whole playout.** (14,288 open-sheet games / 192,912 decision points, the same denominator as the item and seed scans.) **THE BOARD RECORDS THE AGE AND THE SETTER'S ROCK, NEVER A DURATION**, and that is the whole design: how long a weather lasts is `MEDI.weatherTurns(w, item, TAGS)`, the one function `applyMegaWeather` already calls, and a second copy of that arithmetic on the board is the FACTS-ARE-GLOBAL breach that gave the two weather paths different answers in the first place. `setWeather` stamps `weatherSince`/`weatherRock` with no caller change at all, and `noteMove` passes the user's item because the rock is the SETTER's. `rollout_leaf.applyFieldClock` — a new seam beside `applySideState`, NOT a new argument on `applyField`, because seven callers build the field object by hand and every one of them would otherwise grow the same two lines — sets `weatherT`/`terrainT` at both leaves. **AN EXPIRED WEATHER IS DELETED, NOT COUNTED DOWN TO ZERO**, since zero is the engine's word for "runs forever" and handing one over is the bug spelled differently. The terrain half was already representable and simply never read (`board.fieldLeft`, there since #249). **NOT FIT-INVALIDATING**: `board.weather` keeps meaning exactly what it meant, so `deadWeather` and every weather-scaled feature are byte-identical — that the board's weather never expires FOR THOSE FEATURES is a real and separate defect, filed as **#276** rather than fixed here. Two declared limits, both counted: the store's `w` event and the live `|-weather|` line name no setter, so a rock-extended weather laid by an ABILITY is clocked at its base length and ends three turns early (`weatherCounters.setRockless`) — erring SHORT, the direction #270 already chose; and a board that never saw the weather start is left alone rather than expired, because inventing an expiry is the same class of error as never having one. **VERIFIED BY: `node tests/test-board-clock-power.js`** — shown RED on two deliberate breaks (`weatherLeft` always null; the naive re-declaration), with `deadWeather` 1 -> 0 across the boundary and a weather-scaled damage column as the two BEHAVIOURAL arms | closed 2026-08-17 — SEARCH; gate tests/test-board-clock-power.js; artifact data/feature-shift.json |

| #278 | **THE PAIRED ARGMAX RUN — REACH BECOMES EFFECT, AND IT IS 47.3%. RUN AND CLOSED 2026-08-14 BY SEARCH.** R13 through R16 each published a PREVALENCE and each said, correctly, that it is a CEILING ON REACH and not an effect — a body priced holding an item it no longer holds may still be the same argmax. This is the run that converts one into the other. **Six arms, each a COMMIT**, compiled with `Module._compile` into `require.cache` (#254’s technique, because three other agents were live in the tree), so the delta between consecutive arms is ONE landing and attribution is a subtraction rather than an assertion; the simulator, the dex, the tags, the priors, the engine data and the weights all come from one frozen release and are byte-identical across every arm. **Over 131 paired decision points under common random numbers keyed on the CANDIDATE’S IDENTITY rather than its index, the whole of the seed work moves MILTANK’s argmax on 62 of them — 47.3% — and moves MAG’s argmax on ZERO.** Per landing: **#244 6.1%**, **#254 0.0%**, **#247/#248/#249/#250 46.6%**, **#271 0.0%**, **#267/#268/#269/#270 9.9%**. **THE PAIRED NULL IS ZERO AND THIS RUN DEMONSTRATES IT THREE TIMES** — the identical-dice control (0/131), and two arm pairs whose change provably cannot reach these positions (#254, #271). **THE UNPAIRED FIGURE IS NOT THE COMPARATOR**: the same arm against itself with only the dice changed disagrees on **62.6%**, which is R3’s finding from another direction and is why the run is paired at all. **THE ONE THING CRN CANNOT SETTLE IS SWEPT RATHER THAN ARGUED**: common random numbers pair the SEED, not the TRAJECTORY, so some flips are a rerouted die — the rate falls 53.4% → 47.3% → 41.2% across n=12 → 40 → 100, so an 8.3x budget costs 12 points and the residual is real and is not most of it. **WHETHER THE NEW CHOICE IS BETTER IS UNMEASURED, AND THE NUMBER SAYS SO RATHER THAN THE PROSE**: `head`’s pick is worth +9.70 points over `pre`’s by `head`’s own leaf, and the identical statistic on the UNPAIRED control — where neither pick is better by construction — is +7.96. That gap is argmax selection bias, it shrinks with budget the way selection bias does, and it carries no direction. **A FLIP IS NOT A WIN; the thing that answers “better” is an SPRT and this is not one.** **#271 AND #269 CONTRIBUTE EXACTLY ZERO AND THAT IS THE HARNESS, NOT THE FIX**: the decision points come from `joint_rows.js`’s replay, which calls neither `board.noteItem` (whose one caller is the live protocol reader) nor `board.startVolatile` — so every rate here is a FLOOR on the live board, and closing that is PRIORITIES 13e and is MEASURE’s | closed 2026-08-14 — SEARCH; generator engine/argmax_paired.js; artifacts data/argmax-paired.json + data/argmax-paired-n12.json + data/argmax-paired-n100.json |
| #279 | **THREE MOVES ALWAYS CRIT IN THIS FORMAT AND WE ROLL A DIE FOR THEM, AND ON AN ORDINARY MOVE THE AUTHORITY ROLLS WHERE WE DO NOT — 2026-08-14.** Found by the middle arm before the middle arm works, which is the argument for building it. Its address dump on a voided game: the authority draws `dmg|flowertrick` and NO crit; we draw `dmg|flowertrick` AND `crit|flowertrick`. On the next move it inverts — the authority draws `acc|lowkick`, `crit|lowkick`, `dmg|lowkick` and we draw `acc` and `dmg` with **no crit at all**. **DERIVED AND FILTERED TO THE REGULATION: exactly three legal moves carry `willCrit: true` — Storm Throw, Flower Trick, Frost Breath.** Wicked Blow and Surging Strikes carry it and are `isNonstandard` here. A guaranteed crit that ROLLS is wrong on every use — sometimes we do not crit on a move that always does, which is a DAMAGE error rather than a narration one, and it is invisible to the pinned corners because both arms force the crit die to a constant. **THE SECOND HALF IS THE MORE INTERESTING ONE AND IS NOT DIAGNOSED**: Low Kick has `willCrit: false` and `critRatio: 1`, so the authority rolls an ordinary 1-in-24 and we appear to skip it entirely. Either our crit is computed somewhere the address does not see, or a class of moves never rolls one. **ALSO DERIVED, AND UNCHECKED HERE: 14 legal moves carry `critRatio: 2`** — Crabhammer, Night Slash, Shadow Claw, Air Cutter, Aqua Cutter, Blaze Kick, Cross Chop, Cross Poison, Drill Run, Leaf Blade, Psycho Cut, Sky Attack, Stone Edge, Triple Arrows — and nobody has confirmed we double their rate. **PROBE ALL THREE SHAPES**: a guaranteed crit must crit 100/100 with no draw; a `critRatio: 2` move must crit at roughly twice base; an ordinary move must draw exactly once. A probe on one shape passes on an engine that has the other two backwards. **CORRECTED AND LARGELY RETRACTED 2026-08-14, BY THE PROBE THIS ROW ASKED FOR.** The row's central claim — *"a DAMAGE error rather than a narration one"* — is **FALSE**, and it was typed from a reading of the address dump rather than measured. Through `dmgRange`, the function the battle loop and the pricer both call: **Storm Throw 34, Flower Trick 39, Frost Breath 304, and asking for a NON-crit returns those same numbers**, because `dmgRangeOneHit` derives `_critHere` from `critChance(...) === 1` itself and the loop deliberately skips re-pricing a rate of exactly 1 so the 1.5 is never applied twice. **The control is what makes that readable**: `flamethrower 56 -> 84` and `surf 75 -> 112` in the same call, so the flag is not being ignored. **THE `critRatio: 2` HALF ALSO CHECKS OUT** — all 14 carry `{"critRatio":2}` in `data/tags.json` and `CRIT_BY_STAGE=[1/24,1/8,1/2,1]` is the correct gen-9 ladder, so they price at stage 1. **WHAT SURVIVES IS NOT AN ENGINE DEFECT**: we consume `_R.crit()` unconditionally where the authority short-circuits `willCrit` and never draws, which costs an ADDRESS in the middle arm's differential and nothing in a game — and the draw is unconditional on purpose, so a Shell Armor arm and a plain arm read the same stream. The Low Kick half is most likely the middle arm's own category assignment, which `docs/MEDICHAM-SPRINT-NOTES.md` had already recorded as the open suspect; it is a claim about the instrument until an instrument that works says otherwise. **AND THE COVERAGE WAS NOT ABSENT, IT WAS DEAD**: `tests/probe_red_demo.js` pins alwaysCrit in four places and is #273 — red with 31 stale reversals. A mechanic pinned only in an instrument that does not run is not pinned, so the probe was added to `tests/test-mechanics.js`, which does | corrected 2026-08-14 — NOT an engine defect; live probe in tests/test-mechanics.js; residue is the middle-arm address gap, which belongs to the differential harness and not to MEDICHAM |
| #280 | **NO MILTANK MEASUREMENT IN THIS PROJECT'S HISTORY HAS EVER RUN ON MORE THAN ONE CORE, AND THE RUNNER HAD NO IDEA HOW BIG ITS OWN WORKERS ARE — FOUND AND FIXED 2026-08-14 WHILE RUNNING #57's R4.** Three compounding costs, each a tool that already existed and was not reached for. **(1) `--conc` IS NOT PARALLELISM.** `mew.js:967` is `Promise.all` over async workers in ONE process — no `worker_threads`, no `child_process`. The simulator is synchronous and CPU-bound, so in-process concurrency overlaps nothing and only multiplies GC pressure. `mew_farm.js`'s header has said **"CONC MUST BE 1"** since 2026-07-25 with the measured table (8 procs × conc 4 = 11 games/s; 8 × conc 1 = **38**), and R4's arms were launched at `--conc 6` in a single process anyway. Measured: probe at conc 2 = 34.25 s/game, real run at conc 6 = 34.7 s/game — **identical, and that evidence was in hand before the run started.** One core of sixteen, 13% load, 8h28m for 880 games. **(2) MILTANK WAS NOT FARMABLE AT ALL.** `mew_farm`'s unknown-flag guard correctly REFUSED `--miltank2` rather than dropping it (the 2026-07-31 `--opponent-model` lesson working exactly as designed), and nobody had ever added the miltank family to the lists — so every MILTANK number ever published came from a single process. Added `miltank`, `miltank2`, `miltank-no-defer`, `no-raw`, `closed-sheets`, `explain` to `BOOL_FLAGS` and `miltank-n`, `miltank-foe`, `miltank-preview-n`, `mega` to **BOTH** the forwarding list and `KNOWN_VALUE` — **the asymmetry is a trap and is now commented**: `KNOWN_VALUE` only silences the error, so a value flag added there alone reproduces the exact bug the guard exists to stop. **(3) `sprt.js --watch` EXISTS AND WAS NOT USED.** Run 1 decided at 625 games of 2,326 (73.1% wasted); run 2 decided at **84 of 880 (90.5% wasted)**. Combined, run 2's 8.5 hours needed about five minutes. **THE RAM GUARD IS THE PART THAT IS A CHECK RATHER THAN A LESSON.** `mew_farm`'s default is core-based (0.75 × cpus, capped 16) and picks 12 on this 13.3 GB machine; a MILTANK worker measured **663–867 MB fresh and 1,948 MB after an 8h28m run**, so 12 workers is ~10 GB before growth. The machine swaps, and **a swapping desktop does not look slow, it looks FROZEN** — which is the force-quit Will reported twice, misdiagnosed both times as something outside this repo (orphan processes, then vmmem at 3.8 GB; vmmem measured 0.12 GB the second time and was not the cause). New `engine/ram_cap.js` — its own module because `mew_farm` spawns at require time, so a test importing the cap from there would launch the farm as a side effect. `tests/test-farm-ram-guard.js` pins the regression, is auto-discovered by `run-all.js`, and **was shown RED on a deliberate break** (`granted 12`, exit 1) before being trusted. It capped its first real run 4 → 2 unprompted and printed *"This run is NARROWER than the one you asked for"*. **VERIFIED BY: `node tests/test-farm-ram-guard.js`** — the register row and the gate that decides it, checked against each other by `engine/register_reality.js` rather than by anyone remembering. | closed 2026-08-14 — engine/ram_cap.js + engine/mew_farm.js + tests/test-farm-ram-guard.js |
| #281 | **R4 RE-RUN, THREE ARMS — MILTANK BEATS MAG, AND MAG'S OWN SWITCH FEATURE COSTS IT ~15 POINTS. RUN 2026-08-14, QUARANTINED ON ARRIVAL.** #57's re-run of the gate `mag_bot.js:786` records as never having happened. **THE THREE ARMS EXIST BECAUSE ONE RUN WOULD HAVE CONFOUNDED TWO CHANGES**: `mag_bot.js:752` forces `switching: SWITCHING || ROLLOUT`, so live MILTANK switches and live MAG does not — a default head-to-head differs in search AND in whether a whole class of move is reachable. `mew.js` does NOT couple them (`:531`/`:532` read the flags only), so the two are separable there and the as-shipped configuration must be set deliberately. **RUN 1, switching alone (2,326 games, seed 1):** MAG with switching OFF takes **65.1% of 398 decisive pairs, 95% CI [60.3, 69.6]**, SPRT accepts H1 after 116 decisive pairs. `magnemite.js:748` says the feature is default-off *"until a switch policy beats not switching, and the number to beat is 81.9%"* and estimates the cost at 10 points; measured here it is worse, and it is a live defect in the shipping player independent of MILTANK. **RUN 2, search alone (880 games):** with BOTH arms switching, MILTANK takes **94.3% of 265 decisive pairs**; SPRT accepts H0 against arm 1 after 30 decisive pairs. **THIS NUMBER MUST BE READ WITH RUN 1 IN HAND** — forcing switching ON to make the comparison fair did not remove a confound, it CRIPPLED the MAG arm, so 94.3% is an upper bound and not the honest margin. **RUN 3, as shipped (in flight):** MAG switching-off vs MILTANK switching-on, each player in its best configuration. At 200 games the raw split was 136–64, a visibly smaller margin, which is the direction run 1 predicts. **THE DECISIVE-PAIR RATE IS ITS OWN FINDING**: 34.2% in run 1 against **60.2%** in run 2 — with MILTANK on the board the POLICY decides the game roughly twice as often as it otherwise does, and nothing about that was implied by "search beats score". **AFFORDABILITY, WHICH IS THE QUESTION R4 DOES NOT ASK**: 9,655 logged decisions, mean **48.9 options and 3,859 ms per decision** (max 24,299 ms), ~4 s on the bring. At 20–30 turns that is 80–200 s against a 7-minute clock, so the search FITS. **BOTH READERS AGREE ON EVERY COUNT** (`--verify`: 2-0 259/259, 0-2 139/139, 15/15, 250/250). **QUARANTINED ON ARRIVAL AND THE FIGURE TRAVELS WITH THE STAMP, NOT A CAPTION**: MEDICHAM has not passed its gate, so the MAGNITUDES are provisional; the direction is not subtle enough for engine error to have invented it. | #282 | **THE LIVE ADAPTER READS A VOLATILE'S DURATION OFF MOVES THIS REGULATION DOES NOT CONTAIN — FILED 2026-08-14 BY SEARCH WHILE CLOSING #277's TRANSLATION.** `engine/magnemite.js:130` builds its duration table with `for (const m of dex.moves.all())` and skips only `!m.exists` — **no `isNonstandard` filter**, which is the walk CLAUDE.md names by name (*"`Dex.forFormat` IS NOT A LEGALITY FILTER"*). Measured on the format rather than argued: the move that shares the heal-block volatile's name is `isNonstandard: 'Past'` and carries `condition.duration: 5`, while the ONE legal carrier in this regulation is Psychic Noise, whose `blocksHealing` tag says **2** — the same 2 Showdown's own `durationCallback` returns for it. So a live board records a heal block as five turns where the game says two, and since #277 that number is now carried into every playout. | #295 | **THE REACH SHELF MIXES TWO UNITS AND SO IS TWO THRESHOLDS WEARING ONE NUMBER — FOUND 2026-08-18.** `engine/quarantine.js`'s `REACH_SHELF` is the integer 25, and it is compared against TEAMS for an ability (denominator 26,232 open-sheet teams) and against CLICKS for a move (denominator 1,259,717). So the same literal means **0.095% of teams** or **0.0020% of clicks** — a move clears the bar at roughly **48x lower relative usage** than an ability. That is not a judgement anyone made; it is an artefact of two usage artifacts having different denominators, and it is invisible because both print as a bare integer beside a name. **The shelf is NOT wrong today and this is not urgent**: Will set it at 25 deliberately (2026-08-18, *"leave it at 25"*) and the five rows holding the mechanics clause shut — `cursedbody` 2,177 teams, `toxicdebris` 1,840, `disable` 1,799 clicks, `regenerator` 1,596, `poltergeist` 1,383 — clear any threshold in the plausible range, so no verdict turns on it. It becomes load-bearing the moment anyone tunes the number, which is exactly when nobody will notice the units. **THE FIX IS A RATE, NOT A BIGGER INTEGER**: express reach as a share of its own denominator and shelve below one rate, so the two populations are asked the same question. Print the denominator beside every row so the unit is never implicit again. Whoever does it must re-derive the shelved/counted split under the new rule and say which rows MOVED, because a re-unitised threshold is a new threshold and the 34/15 split published in CHANGELOG 5.36.0 does not carry across it  **FIXED 2026-08-18 BY MEASURE, AND IT MOVED NOTHING, WHICH IS THE TEST IT HAD TO PASS.** The anchor is unchanged and is now named in its own unit: `REACH_SHELF_CLICKS = 25`, which is where the number has always come from (`tests/roster.js:1517` `USAGE_SHELF_BELOW = 25`, applied at :1522 as `clicks >= USAGE_SHELF_BELOW` — a clicks threshold, ruled on as a clicks threshold; Will, 2026-08-18: *"leave it at 25"*). The common unit is **OCCURRENCES PER STORED GAME**, which both instruments already express and neither has to be reinterpreted to reach: a move's rate is `clicks / click-counts.store_games` (64,846), an ability's is `teams / sheet-usage.sheet_games` (13,116). The teams threshold is DERIVED at the anchor's rate — 25 x 13,116 / 64,846 = **5.06 teams**, so 6 teams is the smallest counting observation — and the comparison is integer cross-multiplication (`n * 64846 < 25 * denom`), so no float boundary can move a row and the move shelf is preserved bit-for-bit at `n < 25`. **THE SPLIT REPRODUCES EXACTLY: 34 counted, 15 shelved, before and after, with no row moving in either direction.** It reproduces because no diverging ability sits between 6 and 24 teams — the lowest counted one is `angerpoint` at 25 — and every shelved row is a move decided by the unchanged anchor. **A RE-UNITISED THRESHOLD IS STILL A NEW THRESHOLD** and the equality is a measured property of today's population rather than of the change: an ability diverging at 6-24 teams used to be shelved and now COUNTS, which is the direction the defect demanded. **ANCHORING THE OTHER WAY DOES NOT REPRODUCE, WHICH IS WHY THE ANCHOR IS NAMED RATHER THAN ASSUMED**: taking the ability figure as the anchor (25 teams = 1.906 per thousand games) puts the move shelf at 124 clicks and shelves twelve currently-counted moves, from `move:ficklebeam` (112) down to `move:attract` (30). Same fix, opposite result. **THE DENOMINATOR NOW TRAVELS WITH EVERY COUNT** — `reachOf` carries `denom`/`denomLabel`, the clause's own summary prints `2177 teams/13,116 open-sheet games`, and `--reach` prints the population and the applicable shelf on every row — because the actual defect was that a bare integer beside a name cannot carry its unit and one header line is not a fix. Two more literals were untangled in the same pass: `coverageClause` reads the CLICKS anchor because its loop walks `click-counts.json.moves` and nothing else, and the decision-impact sample floor became `DECISION_POINTS_FLOOR` — the same 25 in a THIRD unit (paired decision points), which is exactly how one name came to mean three things. | #346 | **THE WHOLE-GAME HARNESS MANUFACTURED DIVERGENCES ON A DOUBLE KO, AND CARD 25 WAS ENTIRELY THAT — FOUND AND FIXED 2026-08-22, WITH NO REGISTER ROW UNTIL NOW.** `engine/game_differential.js` mirrors medicham2's actives into Showdown's forced-switch replacement choice and the fallback had **no memory of what the other slot just took**, so on a double KO on a side down to its last usable body it sent `"switch 4, switch 4"`. Showdown refused it — *"The Pokemon in slot 4 can only switch in once"* — **eight times**, and **the return value of `battle.choose` was DISCARDED on that path**, so every refusal was swallowed; `requestState` stayed `switch` and the next loop reported *"showdown stopped asking for a move"*. `chooseAction` already carried a `claimed` set; this path did not. **With the choice repaired the two engines agree on ALL 136 LINES of that game and both end it with the same winner. Throwing there would have moved a REAL engine divergence into the instrument's error column, so the mirror has a third answer, `cannot`, and the game stops with a named end reason AFTER the turn's bookkeeping runs — load-bearing, because the comparator call four statements later is what records the earlier, real divergence. Counted apart as `forced_switch_unmirrorable`, expected non-zero, DELIBERATELY NOT GATED. `choices_refused` counts one `battle.choose()` the authority returned false for: 0 across 129 pinned games, 0 in a 12-game full-driver run, **exactly 1 under deliberate sabotage, asserted**. Showdown emits `-end ... Infestation [partiallytrapped] [silent]` twice and `-end ... Throat Chop` once, and we emit nothing. **The state behind them IS compared** — `board_state.js:661` reads `trapped_by_move`, and the file notes the victim's half is *"the half that decides whether a switch is legal"* — **but the comparator's own proof is not clean**: `probe_bench_plants.js` lists **a MOVE TRAP counter off by one** among its 19 applied-and-not-caught plants (7 of 8 pairs catch it), which is #314's second hole. | #343 | **A FAILED ROOST LEAVES THE USER STILL FLYING ON THE AUTHORITY AND GROUNDED HERE, AND IT IS BOARD-MATERIAL — STAGED AND DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED 2026-08-22.** Found while landing #342's `null`-against-`false` classification and stated in that commit's own account, with no register row until now. We emit the `-fail ... heal` line correctly and **THEN ALSO the `-singleturn`**; the authority emits NEITHER, because `runMoveEffects` marks the target `false` and `selfDrops` skips false targets. The consequence is not the two lines: it is that the Roost user's Flying type is suppressed here on a turn the authority leaves it Flying, so **a Ground move that should be refused connects, and every damage and speed read for that turn is taken against the wrong type**. Left undone on purpose — the rule in this repository is one family per pass, and the Roost family wants its own hand list rather than a free ride inside the Encore/Yawn batch. **WHAT WOULD DECIDE IT**: stage a Roost that fails (a user already at full HP) on both engines and assert (a) neither line is emitted and (b) a Ground move into that user is refused on both. Source `docs/CARD-REVIEW-2026-08-22.md` §E3 and commit `11bab14`. Adjacent: #147 wired Roost's grounding in the first place; #242 records that Roost is residual order 25, announces nothing, and gives its type back TWENTY orders above the Grassy Terrain heal | open — engine DEFECT (ENGINE; staged 2026-08-22, filed by MEASURE, fix deliberately deferred) |

| #342 | **WE EMITTED AN EXTRA `-fail` ON THE PHAZE REFUSAL BECAUSE WE HAD NO MODEL OF `null` AGAINST `false` — FOUND AND FIXED 2026-08-22, AND THE FIX HAD NO REGISTER ROW UNTIL NOW.** Showdown's move pipeline carries a FOUR-VALUED result (`battle-actions.ts:1156-1170`) and three independent sites branch on it: **`null` means handled, stay silent; `false` means failed, announce it.** We modelled neither, which is why we were **silent where we should speak** (Encore, Yawn) and **speaking where we should be silent** (the phaze refusal, which printed `-activate ... suctioncups` PLUS a `-fail`, where `onDragOut` returns `null` and the authority writes `-fail` only on `hitResult === false`). One root, opposite symptoms. `applyMoveVolatile` now classifies every refusal as FAIL or SILENT and a caller that cannot classify one announces NOTHING. **RED FIRST ON 5 OF 10 ARMS** — Encore into a body that has not moved; a second Encore onto the volatile it just made; Encore into a body whose last move was Encore, which was a STATE bug (we actually applied it); a second Yawn; and the phaze case running the other way. All 10 green, five counters at exact equality. **THE OVER-FIRE PROOF IS THE PART THAT MATTERS**, because over-firing is what got the previous attempt retracted: 355 games, pool pinned identically, arms differing only in `medicham2-browser.js`, ATTRIBUTED BY TRACE DELTA rather than by tally — of the 44 games whose first delta is our failed-move line, **ZERO agreed-then-parted**. Yawn's `onTryHit` half is DELIBERATELY LEFT UNDONE: our `canTakeStatus` is wider than `runStatusImmunity` and includes Safeguard, where the authority is silent, and announcing there is the exact over-fire that cost the last attempt. Ours also printed the raw id `suctioncups` where the authority prints `Suction Cups`. Landed in commit `11bab14`; the account is `docs/CARD-REVIEW-2026-08-22.md` §E3 and `docs/MEDICHAM-SPRINT-NOTES.md`. **This row is filed CLOSED so the fix is not re-discovered, not so it is re-done** | closed 2026-08-22 — ENGINE, commit `11bab14`; filed retrospectively by MEASURE 2026-08-22 because the landing carried no register row |
| #340 | **THE BENCH IS IN A DIFFERENT ORDER FROM THE AUTHORITY'S, AND BOTH OF OUR INSTRUMENTS ARE BLIND TO IT BY CONSTRUCTION — DIAGNOSED 2026-08-22, DIE HYPOTHESIS REFUTED, LANDED AS A DIAGNOSIS WITH NO ROW UNTIL NOW.** The drag divergence was hypothesised to be the die and it is not: both engines roll a uniform die, consume exactly one draw, and under pinned dice draw **the same value at the same address**. What differs is the ORDER of the list the die indexes into. `sim/battle-actions.ts:125-132` **swaps** the outgoing body into the arriving body's party slot; we remove-and-append. **Measured: 34.8% of side-boundaries hold the same bench in a different order, and 0 of 3,118 index positions map to the same body** — zero fixed points, so a uniform index disagrees with probability 1. **INVISIBLE TO BOTH INSTRUMENTS, AND THAT IS THE IMPORTANT PART**: the protocol comparison sees no line, and `board_state.js`'s `partyMap` keys the party BY SPECIES — correctly, because index-keying manufactured a divergence in 123 of 179 games — and the same correct choice makes it blind to WHERE a body is standing. **BLAST RADIUS IS LARGER THAN THE DRAG**: `bringIn`'s default replacement is `_live(bench)[0]`, so a reordered bench moves every faint replacement and every pivot with no explicit target, in rollouts and in `board.js` features. **Measure it; do not assume it neutral.** WHAT WOULD DECIDE IT: a boundary-by-boundary comparison of the two party arrays by INDEX (not by species) on a pinned run, asserting the same body stands at the same index, with the 3,118 positions as the denominator. | #333 | **THE MULTI-HIT DAMAGE PATH WAS NEVER CONVERTED TO THE SIXTEEN-INDEX ROLL, SO #304'S FIX COVERS SINGLE HITS ONLY — PROVED BY ARITHMETIC 2026-08-22 FROM CARDS 38 AND 39.** `Battle#randomizer` is `tr(tr(base * (100 - random(16))) / 100)`: sixteen discrete values between 85% and 100% of the base. Twin Beam is the clean case because both hits are 40 BP, so the base is identical for both. Authority `185 -> 130 -> 75`, hits of **55 and 55** — one base, the same index twice. medicham2 `185 -> 125 -> 75`, hits of **60 and 50**. **No single base can produce both under that scheme**: 60 requires `B >= 60` and 50 requires `trunc(B x 0.85) <= 50`, i.e. `B < 60`. Contradiction. So either our base changes between hits of a constant-BP move or our draw is not the sixteen-index one. **This is the unconverted residue of #304**, whose own status cell already names it — *"8 games survive, candidate is the declared per-hit multi-hit randomizer"* — and a uniform draw WOULD produce 60 and 50 from one base because it can hit any integer in the interval. It also explains Will's observation that multi-hit damage "evens out": a uniform draw is unbiased about the same mean, so totals converge while individual hits scatter. **WHAT WOULD DECIDE IT**: a staged constant-BP two-hit move on both engines under pinned dice, asserting the two hits are equal whenever the authority's two are equal, plus a per-hit support count in the style of #304's `damage_interior` block | open — engine DEFECT (ENGINE; the multi-hit half of #304, which is CLOSED and must not be read as covering it) |

OBSERVED 2026-08-22 BY WILL READING DIVERGENCE CARDS 29 AND 37; NOT PROBED SINCE.** Two defects, filed as one row because they are one derivation. ORDER: `sim/battle-actions.ts:1099` runs `secondaries()` and `:1121` runs `runEvent('DamagingHit')`, so secondaries resolve FIRST; card 29 is Flare Blitz's burn against a Stamina Defense boost in the opposite order here. FREQUENCY: card 37 has the authority laying one Toxic Debris layer per hit of Dual Wingbeat where we lay one after the move completes. **Twenty legal abilities ride this hook** — Aftermath, Cursed Body, Cute Charm, Effect Spore, Electromorphosis, Flame Body, Gooey, Illusion, Innards Out, Justified, Mummy, Poison Point, Rough Skin, Sand Spit, Spicy Spray, Stamina, Static, Toxic Debris, Wandering Spirit, Weak Armor — and several are board-material rather than cosmetic: Aftermath and Innards Out deal damage, so the attacker's HP when the secondary lands differs; Weak Armor changes Speed and therefore turn order; Cursed Body disables a move; and **Mummy overwrites the attacker's ability**, so running it before a secondary the NEW ability would have modified resolves that secondary under the wrong ability entirely. **WHAT WOULD DECIDE IT**: two staged pairs run on both engines — (a) a contact Flare Blitz into a Stamina carrier, asserting the same relative order of the burn and the boost, and (b) a two-hit contact move into a Toxic Debris carrier, asserting TWO layers rather than one. Tyranitar), Klutz `+1` (3), Mimicry `-1` (1, Stunfisk-Galar), Forecast `-2` (4, Castform + formes), Hospitality `-2` (2, Sinistcha and Sinistcha-Masterpiece). **Card 26 is the proof and it runs the wrong way round from speed**: Torkoal is base-20 Speed and Sinistcha base-70, yet Drought fired FIRST on the authority because Hospitality carries `-2`. We put the faster body first and got the reverse. Same family as the Intimidate-vs-Drizzle card. **WHAT WOULD DECIDE IT**: a staged entry pair on both engines with a Hospitality carrier faster than a weather setter, asserting the weather setter resolves first; plus the derived membership printed before wiring, because a name-matched implementation of five carriers is the shape this repository bans. Proved live with a knob that moves — the FASTEST body on the board resolves LAST carrying Hospitality (-2) and FIRST carrying Drizzle (0), both engines agreeing on both arms — with `MEDSEEN.switchInPrioritySeparated = 60` over 120 real games, so the layer is not decoration. OBSERVED 2026-08-22 BY WILL READING CARDS 28 AND 34; NOT PROBED SINCE.** `this.add('faint', pokemon)` lives inside `faintMessages()` (`sim/battle.ts:2549`), which `runAction` calls at `:2832` **after the action finishes**, so Showdown completes the move's own consequences — the recharge flag, Stone Axe's hazard, Fling's item — and announces deaths afterwards. **Two consequences beyond the ordering itself.** (1) `faintMessages(lastFirst = false, …)`: the `lastFirst` argument is why Final Gambit faints the USER before the target on the authority and the reverse here. (2) It also explains a wording difference for free — `Pokemon#toString()` returns `p2a: Name` while active and bare `p2: Name` once not, and `faintMessages` sets `isActive = false` at `:2563`, so the authority's `-hitcount` names an already-dead body (`p2: Azumarill`) where ours names a live one (`p2a: Azumarill`). One root, two visible symptoms. **LIKELY WORTH MORE THAN ITS CARD COUNT**: a faint announced early shifts every line after it, so games currently filed under other classes may be downstream of this one. Imposter (#320) predicted ~10 and delivered 13 for exactly that reason. **WHAT WOULD DECIDE IT**: stage a lethal Stone Axe and a lethal Final Gambit on both engines and assert the `|faint|` line's position relative to the hazard line and to the target's faint. OBSERVED 2026-08-22 BY WILL READING CARD 30; NOT PROBED SINCE.** The authority's residual case runs `fieldEvent('Residual')`, then `add('upkeep')`, then `faintMessages()`, and only then `eachEvent('Update')` — and Sitrus hooks `onUpdate`, so it eats after the turn-end marker AND after deaths are processed. We eat during the residual sequence. **Two ways that moves the board rather than the narration.** (a) A body that residual damage would kill is dead before the berry can be eaten on the authority; eating mid-sequence heals it above zero and it lives. (b) With several residuals in sequence, eating mid-sequence gives a larger HP pool to every residual that follows — body at 20, poison 15 then sandstorm 7: authority 20 -> 5 -> 0 DEAD, ours 20 -> 5 -> berry 36 -> 29 ALIVE. **THE RESIDUAL ORDER ITSELF IS CHECKED AND CORRECT AND IS NOT PART OF THIS ROW**: `data/residual-order.json` carries sandstorm at order 1 / `onFieldResidual` and Leftovers at order 5 / subOrder 4, derived from the authority, and the simulator reads the numbers from that file rather than hardcoding them (#221, #242). | #355 | **TWO TAILWINDS EXPIRING ON THE SAME TURN COME OUT IN THE OPPOSITE ORDER, AND #242 SETTLED WHERE IN THE WALK RATHER THAN WHICH SIDE — 2 OF THE 121, FILED 2026-08-22 BY MEASURE.** One cause with `n=2` (release `13ba05093aa3`, 961 games, generated 2026-08-22T19:15Z): `ordering :: \|-sideend\|p2:\|tailwind <> \|-sideend\|p1:\|tailwind`. **#242 IS CLOSED AND DOES NOT COVER IT**: it placed Tailwind at `onSideResidualOrder 26 / onSideResidualSubOrder 5` and landed ten clocks inside the residual walk, on BOTH sides — an order that is by construction identical for the two sides, so it cannot decide which of them announces first. **IT IS NOT A DIE.** `engine/game_differential.js:2854` installs `battle.prng.shuffle = ARM.shuffle`, the identity, in every arm, so the authority's `speedSort` tie resolution is deterministic on input order here and the two `-sideend` lines are directly comparable. A Side holder has no speed, so `comparePriority`'s middle term cannot separate them either and the answer is whatever order `findSideEventHandlers` collects the two sides in. **WHAT WOULD DECIDE IT**: Tailwind up on both sides with the same expiry turn, assert which `-sideend` lands first, and read the authority's collector order rather than inferring it  **DERIVED 2026-08-24 BY ENGINE, AND IT NEEDS A RULING RATHER THAN A FIX — CARD 1 OF `docs/_reports/2026-08-24-ordering-cards.md`.** The two `-sideend` lines are tied on **ALL FIVE** of the authority’s sort keys: `resolvePriority` (`sim/battle.ts:993-999`) fills the fifth key, `effectOrder`, only for `SwitchIn` and `RedirectTarget` — an end-of-turn side clock never gets one — so `speedSort` hands the pair to `prng.shuffle` (`:456`) and **A COIN DECIDES ON THE AUTHORITY**. Staged both ways: with the shuffle pinned to the identity the authority says p1 first, reversed it says p2 first, and **both Tailwinds end in both arms**. Our side is `residualOrder` (`engine/medicham2-browser.js:5487`), a stable sort, which always says p1. **IT IS NARRATION, AND THAT WAS CHECKED RATHER THAN ASSUMED**: the residual handler list is speed-sorted ONCE before the walk (`sim/battle.ts:505`), so a Tailwind ending cannot reorder anything after it; nothing between the two lines reads side state; and the speed consequence is not applied until the next turn’s `commitChoices`. 2 games of 961. **THE QUESTION FOR WILL**: declare this pair IMPOSSIBLE TO COMPARE (the authority itself is flipping a coin), or leave it undeclared and red. **THIS IS NOT THE SAME QUESTION AS #376** — there the tie device is PINNED on both sides and must therefore agree; here the authority’s fifth key is genuinely absent. **See also #422**, the residual sort’s own defect, which is a different claim **THE CARD'S DERIVATION WAS VERIFIED 2026-08-24 AND THE ROW STILL WAITS ON ONE WORD FROM WILL — MEASURE.** Both halves of the card were checked rather than accepted. (1) The authority's fifth sort key really is absent here: `resolvePriority` fills `effectOrder` only for `SwitchIn` and `RedirectTarget`, so an end-of-turn side clock never gets one and the tied pair really does reach `prng.shuffle` — staged both ways, identity says p1 first and reversed says p2 first, and BOTH Tailwinds end in both arms. (2) The consequence really is narration: the residual handler list is speed-sorted ONCE before the walk (`sim/battle.ts:505`), so a Tailwind ending cannot reorder anything after it; nothing between the two lines reads side state; and the speed consequence is not applied until the next turn's `commitChoices`. **NOBODY HERE MAY DECLARE IT, AND THAT IS THE POINT OF LEAVING IT OPEN.** A declaration SUBTRACTS a row from the open-defect clause, and this project has already had a live turn-order defect sitting under a heading reading "nothing to fix" — the withdrawal recorded in #376. The two options stay exactly as the card put them: declare the pair INCOMPARABLE because the authority itself is flipping a coin, or leave it undeclared and red. 2 games of 961. **SHELVED 2026-08-24 ON WILL'S RULING — DEFERRED, NOT DECLARED.** Will: *"tailwind coming out in the wrong order doesnt matter, put it into the closet with that note and move on."* His reasoning, checked against the derived residual order rather than accepted: Tailwind resolves at **order 26**, after weather (1), poison (9), burn (10) and Perish Song (24), so **no damage remains to land**; the two Tailwind handlers are **adjacent in the list**, so nothing runs between them that could read the difference; the handler list is speed-sorted **once** before the walk (`sim/battle.ts:507`), so an expiry inside the walk cannot reorder anything after it; and the speed consequence is not applied until the next turn's choices, identically on both sides. **Which Tailwind ends first therefore cannot affect a game.** **THIS IS A DEFERRAL AND NOT A DECLARATION, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT.** It remains a real, deterministic narration divergence that we have chosen not to fix, quoted and dated above. It is deliberately **NOT** added to the declared kinds in `engine/quarantine.js` — a deferred row asserts that something IS wrong, so it must never be subtracted from the divergence count; it stays UNDECLARED, red, and named on every run, which is exactly the behaviour that stops a chosen deferral becoming a quiet pass. It is narration, so it already sits outside the board-material bar Will set on 2026-08-22, and shelving it moves no gate figure. **TWO CORRECTIONS TO THIS ROW'S OWN DERIVATION — 2026-08-24 BY MEASURE. THE TEXT ABOVE IS LEFT STANDING AS DATED HISTORY AND IS WRONG ON BOTH POINTS.** (1) **A COIN DOES NOT DECIDE IT.** The account above ends *"A COIN DECIDES ON THE AUTHORITY"*. It does not: with `prng.shuffle` held at the identity — which is what `game_differential.js` installs in EVERY arm — the authority still reverses the pair on the right board. **The reversal is deterministic**, so the pair is comparable, and IMPOSSIBLE TO COMPARE was correctly refused as a filing for it. (2) **THE REMAINING CAUSE IS THE HANDLER LIST, NOT THE SORT.** The speed sort is now correct at all four sites (#422). What our walk cannot reproduce is the authority's full residual handler LIST: the authority's selection sort **swaps** rather than shifts, so sorting the front of the list displaces the tail, and every held item adds a handler that participates in that displacement.
